### Page 1

堅調なAI 需要で大幅増益が視野 
投資増につながるかが焦点に

○ 製造業の業績見通しは大幅に改善している。輸出向けを中心とするAI関連財の需要拡大が背景。
2026年度は好業績が期待され、二桁増益が視野に入る 
○ AI関連財の増産を背景に、国内製造業の設備不足感は2025年後半以降強まっている。足元では関
連業種の機械受注が増加しており、AI需要拡大が国内設備投資に波及する兆しがみられる 
○ 設備投資は緩やかな拡大が息長く続く見通し。ただし、米国でデータセンター建設規制が実施さ
れれば、輸出・業績・設備投資の好循環が逆回転に転じるリスクも

１．企業景況感は AI 関連の製造業を中心に改善 
AIの学習・利用に必要なデータセンター投資や半導体需要が世界的に堅調であることを背景に、日
本の製造業の景況感は幅広く改善している。日銀短観（2026年9月調査）では、大企業・製造業の業況
判断DIが6月調査から改善した（図表1）。業種別では、半導体や電子部品などを含む電気機械や、半
導体製造装置を含む生産用機械の改善が際立つ。そのほか、窯業・土石製品、非鉄金属、半導体材料・
素材を扱う化学などでも改善がみられた。ロイター短観（10月調査）でも、「半導体関連市場の需要
が強い」との声が散見され、AI関連需要の堅調さがうかがえる。 
AI関連財の輸出が拡大し、足元で企業の業績見通しは大きく改善している。企業業績の改善を国内
の需要拡大につなげるうえで、次の焦点となるのが設備投資である。企業業績の改善に比べて、投資
図表 1 業況判断 DI 図表 2 日本の AI 関連財輸出額

2

などの内需への恩恵はまだ明確ではなく、今後設備投資の増加として発現する可能性が期待される。 
２．AI 関連財輸出は好調。2026 年度の製造業は二桁増益が視野に 
世界的なAIサービスの普及に伴い、データセンターや関連機器への投資が拡大している。大規模な
データセンターを保有・運営し、クラウドサービスやAI向けの計算基盤を提供するIT企業、とりわけ
ハイパースケーラーと呼ばれる大規模事業者を中心に、高水準の設備投資を継続する見通しが示され
ていることを踏まえると、AI関連投資の高い伸びは当面持続する見込みである。こうしたなか、モデ
ルの演算を担う処理装置のほか、大量のデータを蓄える記憶装置、サーバー同士をつなぐ通信ケーブ
ル、電力を安定供給する電源装置などといった財において投資需要が高まっている。日本企業が供給
する財についても、電子部品・機器への需要が増加しているほか、AI向け半導体の増産投資は日本の
半導体製造・検査装置や材料への需要を押し上げている。 
図表2は、輸出全体から、WTOの定義に従ってAI関連財を抽出したものだ。AI関連財輸出は2025年終
盤以降加速しており、8月時点では前年比＋29.1％と高い伸びになった。主因は半導体・電子部品で、
旺盛な需要のもとで半導体価格が大幅に上昇し、輸出金額を押し上げている。また、半導体製造・検
査機器や、データセンターに組み込まれる機械・部材、半導体の製造に用いられる原材料・素材など、
幅広いAI関連財が輸出増に寄与している。 
輸出額の高い伸びを受け、製造業では業績見通しが改善している。日銀短観（9月調査）の2026年度
経常利益計画（全規模・製造業）は、6月調査時点から大幅に上方修正され、前年度比＋11.6％の増益
が見込まれる（図表3）。特に電気機械では、上期の堅調な輸出実績を反映して、経常利益計画が大き
く上方修正された。このほか、業況判断DIの改善が目立った業種でも同様の傾向がみられた。堅調な
AI関連需要が下期も続けば、製造業では大幅増益が期待される。

３．AI 関連業種の投資需要は増勢加速の兆しも 
今後の実質GDPへの影響を見通すうえでの焦点は、企業収益の拡大が設備投資を中心とした内需の押
し上げにどの程度繋がるかだ。この点、AI需要の追い風を受け好業績が見込まれることを踏まえれば、
設備投資の伸びはやや物足りなさが否めない。日銀短観（9月調査）によると、製造業の2026年度設備
投資計画は直近5年間では最も低い伸びに留まっている（図表4）。建材や生産設備の価格上昇、金利
上昇に伴う資金調達コストの増加が投資負担となり、好業績のもとでも計画の積み増しを抑えている
可能性がある。 
もっとも、設備投資需要は計画対比で伸び余地がある。AI関連財を中心に増産が進むなか、能力増
強投資の必要性が高まっているためだ。AI関連財の生産は2025年秋以降、はっきりとした増加傾向が
続いている（図表5）。生産・営業用設備判断DI（全規模・製造業）も2025年後半から低下しており、
製造業の設備不足感は強まっている（図表6）。 
実際、AI需要の拡大が今後の設備投資を押し上げる兆しもみられる。図表7は、設備投資の先行指標
とされる機械受注（国内需要向け）を需要者別に分解し、価格変動の影響を取り除いたものだ。これ
をみると2025年夏場以降、情報通信機械、電気機械、はん用・生産用機械といった業種からの受注が
急増していることがわかる。結果として、半導体製造装置をはじめとするAI・データセンター関連品
目では受注残高が積み上がっている（図表8）。 
ただし、機械受注が設備投資として顕在化するまでにはタイムラグを伴うとみられる。そもそも、
受注残高が積み上がっている背景には、旺盛な需要だけでなく、供給面の制約による影響があると考
えられる1。このため、設備投資の増加ペースは緩やかなものにとどまる公算が大きいとみているが、
その分、今後は息の長い投資拡大局面が続くだろう。

図表 5 鉱工業生産 図表6 生産・営業用設備判断DI

### Page 4

4

４．今後は政府の投資支援策も民間投資を後押し。米国の規制強化リスクには要注意 
以上のように、先行きの設備投資は、設備不足感の強まるAI関連産業の投資需要に支えられ、緩や
かに増勢を強めると見込まれる。 
また今後は、政府の政策支援が、投資コストの負担軽減を通じて、設備投資を後押しするとみられ
る。7月末に始動した政府の「大胆な投資促進税制」では、一定の要件を満たす国内投資について、即
時償却や税額控除の適用を認める減税措置が設けられた。さらに、2027年度予算の編成では、特別予
算枠として『「強く豊かな日本」投資枠』を設け、民間投資を促す支援策が検討されている。具体的
な支援策が整えば、複数年度にわたる投資計画の実行を支えよう。 
なお、11月3日に投開票された米国中間選挙の結果を受け、米国におけるデータセンター投資が下振
れるリスクにも触れておきたい。同選挙では、データセンター建設計画の多いテキサス州やジョージ
ア州の州知事選などにおいて、建設の一時停止を掲げる民主党候補が勝利した。米国では、データセ
ンター建設規制に関して州政府や地方自治体が大きな権限を持つ。 
実際、市場ではAI関連企業の業績・投資下振れが意識され、グローバルにハイテク銘柄の株価が急
落した。仮に今後、米国でデータセンター建設が規制されれば、日本でも半導体・電子部品や製造装
置などの輸出が減少し、企業業績が下押しされる可能性がある。その際、AI関連財が減産に転じて設
備不足感が弱まれば、日本国内における能力増強投資の需要も後退しかねない。新知事の任期開始お
よび新議会の招集は2027年1月だが、当面の米国政治動静については注意深くみていく必要があろう。

1 なお、供給制約に起因して、資本財の輸入浸透度（国内供給に占める輸入品の割合）が一層上昇する可能性には留意が必要で
ある。今後、国内の供給余力が限られるなかで投資需要が一層輸入品に向かえば、国産資本財への需要が減退し、投資需要増に
よる GDP の押し上げ効果が限られるリスクがある。

### Page 2

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引

1

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國服務業與內需維持擴張，通膨黏著與中東風險牽動政策前景 
經濟 
⚫ 9 月美國 ISM 服務業 PMI 由 55.4 降至 54.9，連續第 27 個月維持擴張。
商業活動及新訂單雖較前月回落，仍保持穩健成長；就業重返擴張區間，
內需與勞動市場仍具韌性。惟價格指數續升創高，出口與進口動能轉弱，
後續仍須關注通膨壓力及外需變化。 
⚫ 8 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 1,056 億美元，較上月增加 13.7%。
出口 3,152 億美元、進口 4,208 億美元，分別月增 1.4%及 4.3%。出口成
長主要受非貨幣黃金、原油及半導體帶動，進口則以原油、黃金及資本
財增加為主。累計前 8 月貿易逆差 5,570 億美元，較去年同期縮減 19.9%，
惟近期進口增幅高於出口，使逆差再度擴大。 
⚫ 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要顯示，AI 投資持續帶動經濟與就業動能，勞動市
場仍具韌性；惟去通膨進展有限，核心服務通膨及成本轉嫁壓力仍存。
全體官員支持升息 1 碼，傾向維持限制性政策，並關注 AI 融資、公債
市場波動及網路安全等金融風險。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 近期美伊緊張情勢再升溫，川普改口不排除選前對伊朗採取軍事行動，
伊朗則持續尋求重啟談判；同時葉門青年運動頻繁襲擊沙國目標，加劇
中東安全風險。若衝突擴大，恐影響荷姆茲與曼德海峽能源運輸，推升
油氣價格並增添全球經濟下行風險。 
⚫ 美國啟動 337 調查，針對 AI 伺服器垂直供電系統（VPD）相關專利侵
權爭議展開審查，鴻海、台達電、廣達等約 20 家業者列名。由於涉及
AI 伺服器關鍵供電技術，若最終認定侵權，相關產品恐面臨輸美限制，
對供應鏈營運帶來影響。 
短評 
⚫ 美國服務業與內需仍具韌性，AI 投資亦支撐經濟動能，惟貿易逆差擴
大、成本壓力升高及通膨黏著，使聯準會仍需維持偏緊縮立場。加上美
伊與中東局勢升溫，能源價格與金融市場波動風險上升，後續景氣不確
定性仍高。

歐 
元 
區 
法國殖利率短期內恐難降溫 
經濟 ⚫ 德國 8 月工業生產月增率為 2%。 ⚫ 法國 8 月工業生產月增率為-0.3%。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 西班牙政府宣布於 11 月 29 日提前進行大選。 
⚫ 法國 10 年期公債殖利率高出德國 130 個基點以上，主權風險升溫。 
⚫ 歐中談判最終達成 16 項共識，依據歐盟發布聲明，中國將自主減少混
合動力車對歐盟的出口量 5 成以上，雙方同意降低部分產品 MFN 稅率，
預期 2027 年 3 月雙方將再度舉行貿易會談。 
短評 
⚫ 考量法國財政不確定性較高，短期內德國與法國殖利率差距恐難縮小。 
⚫ 法國製造業在出口需求疲弱及財政支撐相對有限下，景氣復甦力道恐仍
較為溫和，預料德、法兩國經濟及工業活動表現分化的情形短期內仍將
持續。 
日 
本 
薪資成長支撐內需，消費信心持穩 
經濟 ⚫ 8 月薪資年增 3.8%。 ⚫ 9 月消費者信心下降 0.1 點至 35.4。 
短評 ⚫ 雖然通膨預期回升與物價壓力抑制部分消費信心，但薪資與所得成長仍 持續支撐家庭支出，使消費者信心維持溫和復甦趨勢。

一、 美國—美國服務業與內需維持擴張，通膨黏著與中東風險牽動政策前景 
2026 年 9 月美國 ISM 服務業採購經理人指數(Services PMI)為 54.9，較上月
55.4 下降 0.5 個百分點，已連續第 27 個月維持於 50 以上擴張區間，且高於近 12
個月平均值 54.1，服務業景氣仍持續穩健擴張。細項觀察，商業活動指數由 61.7
降至 56.5，新訂單指數由 60.9 降至 59.8，雖較前月回落，但仍維持高檔擴張；就
業指數則由 47.8 回升至 50.1，三個月來首度重返擴張區間。價格指數由 72.6 升
至 74.0，創 2022 年 7 月以來新高。供應商交貨指數由 51.3 升至 53.2，交貨時間
再度延長；庫存指數由 56.7 升至 57.8，企業持續提前備貨以因應未來成本上升風
險。此外，未完成訂單指數升至 56.6，創 2022 年 7 月以來高點，惟新出口訂單
指數由 56.3 大幅降至 46.9，八個月來首度跌入收縮區間，進口指數亦由 56.3 降
至 52.9，外部需求及採購動能均降溫。整體而言，美國服務業景氣仍維持穩健擴
張，內需與就業展現一定韌性，但燃料價格上漲、關稅政策、供應鏈限制及地緣
政治等因素持續推升企業成本，後續仍須關注通膨壓力及外需變化對景氣的影響。 
2026 年 8 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 1,056 億美元，較上月修正後 928
億美元增加 127 億美元，增幅 13.7%。當月出口 3,152 億美元，較上月增加 45 億
美元(+1.4%)；進口 4,208 億美元，較上月增加 172 億美元(+4.3%)。其中，商品貿
易逆差擴大至 1,366 億美元，服務貿易順差則小幅增至 310 億美元。出口成長主
要受工業用品及原材料出口增加 63 億美元帶動，其中非貨幣黃金、原油及燃油
出口增幅較為顯著，資本財出口亦受半導體、電腦及電腦周邊設備需求增加所支
撐；進口方面，工業用品及原材料進口增加 91 億美元，資本財進口增加 62 億美
元，其中原油、非貨幣黃金、半導體及工業機械進口增幅較大。累計 2026 年 1 至
8 月，美國商品及服務貿易逆差為 5,570 億美元，較 2025 年同期減少 1,382 億美
元，縮減 19.9%；同期出口成長 11.8%，進口增加 4.4%，顯示美國對外貿易失衡
情況較去年同期有所改善，惟近期進口增幅明顯高於出口，使貿易逆差再度呈現
擴大趨勢。 
2026 年 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要重點如下：(1)經濟：若干官員認為美國經濟成
長動能有所增強，AI 投資規模與速度持續超乎預期，帶動企業投資與生產力提升；

企業活動則受到企業獲利維持高檔及部分政策因素支持；(2)就業：多數官員表示
勞動市場近期略有改善，就業成長維持穩健，失業率仍處低檔，整體勞動市場大
致符合充分就業狀態，供需風險大致平衡；(3)通膨：官員普遍認為近期去通膨進
展有限，非住房核心服務通膨仍偏高，人力、能源及部分進口成本轉嫁情形仍值
得關注；部分官員擔憂 AI 投資與相關支出擴張，可能推升需求並增加通膨持續
性風險；(4)貨幣政策：鑑於經濟活動持續擴張、勞動市場具韌性且通膨仍高於目
標，全體官員支持升息 1 碼。許多官員認為維持較具限制性的政策立場有助降低
通膨風險，部分官員則認為未來可能仍需進一步緊縮政策，以確保通膨回到目標
水準；(5)金融風險：官員普遍認為金融條件整體仍支持經濟成長，但部分官員關
切 AI 基礎設施快速擴張所衍生的融資風險，以及美國公債市場波動、網路安全
與其他潛在金融穩定風險。 
近期美伊及中東局勢再度升溫，市場對區域衝突擴大的疑慮升高。美國總統
川普由先前宣稱期中選舉前不會攻擊伊朗，改口表示「仍有可能」採取軍事行動，
且美軍已就相關軍事選項進行準備，使美伊對抗風險再度攀升；另一方面，伊朗
雖有意透過斡旋重啟談判，但雙方在核計畫與制裁議題上仍存重大分歧。此外，
葉門青年運動持續升高對沙烏地阿拉伯攻擊，利雅德哈立德國王國際機場一週內
多次遇襲，造成多人死傷，凸顯區域安全風險升高。整體而言，若美伊衝突或代
理人戰事進一步升級，恐衝擊荷姆茲海峽及曼德海峽等能源運輸要道，推升國際
油氣價格、加劇通膨壓力，並增添全球金融市場波動及經濟成長下行風險。 
美國國際貿易委員會(USITC)於 10 月 9 日正式啟動 337 條款調查，針對特定
垂直供電系統(Vertical Power Delivery，VPD)、相關零組件及採用該技術的運算設
備是否涉及專利侵權展開審查。此案由美國電源技術廠商 Vicor Corporation 提出
申訴，主張相關產品侵犯其專利權，並要求發布有限排除令及停止侵權命令，以
限制涉案產品進入美國市場。調查對象涵蓋全球約 20 家企業，包括台灣的鴻海、
台達電、廣達及其美國關係企業，另有中國的立訊精密、工業富聯、德國的英飛
凌等國際大廠列名。 本案聚焦 AI 伺服器及高效能運算領域關鍵的供電架構技術，
該技術有助提升晶片供電效率、降低能源損耗，已廣泛應用於資料中心與 AI 運
算設備。隨著全球 AI 基礎建設投資持續擴大，相關專利布局與授權爭議亦日益
升溫。USITC 指出，此次立案僅代表調查程序正式展開，並不表示已認定侵權，
後續將由行政法官進行聽證及審理，預計於 45 天內訂定調查時程。台灣經濟部

二、歐元區—法國殖利率短期內恐難降溫 
 德國工業生產在 7 月月減 1.2%後，8 月回彈 2%，主要反映部分汽車業者在 7
月提前進行歲修，並且在 8 月陸續復工後，帶動生產及庫存回補需求所致；此外，
受到 8 月萊茵河水位降低，沿岸化學工廠物流運輸受阻，致化工產業產出月減
1.2%為主要拖累因素。儘管工業生產如期反彈，惟工業訂單卻大幅下跌 10.6%，
主要是與軍工產業相關的訂單如飛機、船舶、軍用車輛、武器彈藥等訂單減少，
顯示政府前期大額採購效應逐漸消退；除軍工產業外，其餘產業訂單則大致維持
穩定。 
 法國 8 月工業生產月減 0.3%，為連續第二個月下滑，主要係夏季用電高峰過
後電力產出回落，帶動能源部門生產減少所致。另方面，製造業生產月增 0.3%，
其中造紙、化工及電子電機設備等產業自前期低點回升，部分抵銷汽車等運輸設
備產出月減 2.2%的拖累。不過，法國 9 月 INSEE 新訂單指標由 8 月的-13.8 點降
至-15.8 點，顯示企業接單偏弱，主要反映出口需求疲軟。此外，相較於德國近期
透過國防及基礎建設支出擴大支撐工業生產，法國財政擴張力道相對有限，使製
造業景氣較依賴外部需求，德、法兩國製造業表現因而呈現逐漸分化的趨勢。 
 展望未來，近期歐元區各國殖利率上升，除反映通膨壓力增強、升息預增加
之外，亦部分反映西班牙宣布於 11 月 29 日提前進行大選、法國財政前景不明朗
等政治因素影響。儘管較高的殖利率有助於促使法國政府推動財政整頓，但考量
相關改革涉及福利及退休制度調整等政治敏感議題，不確定性較高，短期內德國
與法國殖利率差距恐難縮小。整體而言，法國製造業在出口需求疲弱及財政支撐
相對有限下，景氣復甦力道恐仍較為溫和，預料德、法兩國經濟及工業活動表現
分化的情形短期內仍將持續。 
三、日本—薪資成長支撐內需，消費信心持穩 
 8 月薪資年增 3.8%，其中經常性薪資年增 3.8%、加班費年增 5.2%、實質薪資
年增 1.5%，主要受惠於春鬥加薪效應延續及通膨壓力較去年趨緩所帶動，有助於
進一步提升民眾購買力，支撐消費與經濟復甦。

9 月消費者信心指數小幅下滑 0.1 點至 35.4，但表現仍優於市場預期。受食品

四、中國—歐中達成貿易談判，外部風險暫時降溫 
中國 9 月外匯存底降至 3.40 兆美元，較前月減少 381 億美元，反映美元升
值、政府以外匯購買黃金儲備，加上歐美殖利率上升，造成中國持有的海外債券
資產出現評價損失，皆為外匯存底下滑的主要原因。惟亦不排除中國外匯流入情
況可能有所轉弱，例如出口成長放緩、進口需求增加，或資本外流規模高於預期
等因素。 
展望未來，近期歐中談判達成 16 項共識，未來中國混合動力車出口至歐盟將
面臨調整壓力，但歐盟進一步升高貿易保護措施的風險已降溫。另一方面，基於
中國維持大額經常帳順差、出口商結匯需求及年底季節性資金流入支撐，預期人
民幣仍有升值空間，並帶動外匯存底回升。 
五、印度及東南亞—世銀上調經濟展望；印度央行近四年來首度升息 
(1)世界銀行 2026 年 10 月《東亞與太平洋地區經濟展望報告》指出，受人工智慧
（AI）相關出口與投資強勁帶動，將 2026 年東亞與太平洋地區（EAP）經濟成長
率預測上修 0.3 個百分點至 4.5%，2027 年與 2028 年將分別放緩至 4.4%與 4.3%，
其中，越南、馬來西亞、泰國 GDP 大幅上調至 7.4%、5.1%及 2.0%。世銀指出，
AI 相關商品占多數經濟體出口增長的一半以上，其中，馬來西亞、菲律賓、泰國
和越南的 AI 出口貢獻率更超過七成，但扣除 AI 相關產品後，區域貿易成長呈現
疲弱或負增長，警告該地區的經濟韌性高度依賴 AI 相關製造業和出口，使其容
易受到全球科技支出逆轉的影響，建議應將 AI 產品生產優勢轉化為廣泛應用，
以提升生產力，並創造更多優質就業機會。此外，美伊戰爭推升能源價格，各國
政府比其他地區更積極因應，也更仰賴補貼，此將增加財政成本並減少外匯存底，
世銀預估中東石油出口要到 2027 年中才會恢復至戰前水準，能源成本上升已對
製造業造成壓力，運輸價格大漲也已打擊消費需求，持續性的補貼可能難以為繼。

(2)印度:印度央行 10/7 貨幣政策會議宣布升息 1 碼至 5.5%，為近四年來首度升
息，貨幣政策立場從「中性」調整為「有節制的緊縮」。央行總裁指出，目前已
有跡象顯示，通膨預期升高，且價格壓力正逐漸擴散至更廣泛的領域。經濟展望
方面，印度內需、投資及基礎建設支出持續支撐景氣，央行將 2026/27 財年經濟
成長率預測由 6.7%上調至 7.1%，另受中東局勢緊張、國際油價上漲、食品價格
攀升及氣候因素影響，將 2026/27 財年 CPI 通膨率預測上調至 5.2%，高於 4%的
中期目標。另外，世界銀行 10 月最新《南亞經濟展望》報告，上修印度 2026/27
財年經濟成長率 0.4 個百分點至 7.1%，反映內需消費、製造業、服務業及出口表
現優於預期，然而，中東衝突導致能源價格上漲、聖嬰現象衝擊農業生產、全球
金融市場波動及資本流動風險，仍可能影響經濟前景。整體而言，印度經濟成長
動能仍穩健，但外部風險及輸入性通膨壓力增加，未來貨幣政策將以維持物價穩
定為優先，並有進一步升息的可能。 
 (3)越南: 2026 年第三季經濟成長年增率達 9.95%，高於上季修正後的 8.81%，並
創 4 年新高，累計前三季經濟成長率達 9.01%。成長動能主要來自製造業與營建
業擴張、外人直接投資持續流入、以及外貿大幅成長。各產業均穩健成長，工業
及建築業成長 12.50%(占 GDP 總值 51.57%)；服務業成長 9.54%(占 43.29%)；農
業成長 4.21%(占 5.14%)。以支出面觀之，民間消費成長 8.96%，固定投資成長
21.39%，貨物和服務出、進口分別成長 23.27%、28.75%。展望未來，越南受惠於
全球企業分散供應鏈、AI 產業需求及政府推動基礎建設與數位轉型，經濟前景仍
偏正向。

9

六、台灣—出口動能強勁，物價壓力與貿易風險並存 
9 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增率升至 2.73%，創近兩年半新高，已連續 5 個
月高於 2%警戒線，主要係受豪雨影響，蔬果供應減少帶動價格上漲，加上汽機
車減稅效應淡化、中秋節落點不同及國際油價維持高檔，共同推升物價。除蔬果
外，外食費、房租、電費及交通相關費用皆持續上漲，顯示物價壓力仍在，但主
計總處認為整體漲勢仍屬溫和，預估 10 月 CPI 將略為回落至約 2.5%。 
9 月出口額達 872.2 億美元，年增 60.9%，其中資通產品出口倍增、電子零組
件年增 45.3%。財政部指出，在 AI 相關投資持續、部分電子產品供應偏緊、新品
上市與旺季拉貨等因素共同挹注下，出口表現明顯升溫；隨著 AI 應用擴大及年
底採購需求增加，全年出口額有望突破 9,000 億美元。 
美國國際貿易委員會（USITC）於 10 月 9 日依據《1930 年關稅法》第 337 條
款，對特定垂直供電系統及相關產品啟動專利侵權調查，其中台達電、廣達及鴻
海旗下相關企業等遭列名。若 337 條款調查最終認定侵權，相關產品可能遭禁止
輸入美國，後續應關注調查結果及對台灣電源供應與伺服器供應鏈的影響。

### Page 2

Earnings revision 
We revise down our 2026 earnings estimates by 8% to factor in 3Q26 results (we revise 
up our 2026E revenue estimates by 1%). We revise down 2027/28E earnings estimates 
by 9%/8% to reflect product mix with higher contribution from the server business (we 
revise up our 2027/28E revenue estimates by 6/6%). We now expect the GM expansion 
to be slower due to the higher server business contribution that is margin dilutive, thus 
revise down GPM by 0.2/0.2/0.2ppt in 2026/27/28E.

Valuation:  
We reiterate our Neutral rating on Inventec with a new 12m TP of NT$56 (from NT$61), 
based on 14.2x 2027E P/E (unchanged), which is the company’s average P/E for the past 
10 years, and factoring in our earnings revision.

### Page 3

Investment thesis and Valuation & Risks

Inventec is an ODM company focusing on the design and manufacturing of PCs, servers, 
and consumer electronics. While we like Inventec’s solid position in the AI server ODM 
market, we are Neutral rated as we believe valuation already reflects near-term 
positives. We model Inventec earnings growth in 2026-28E to be driven by the 
commercial PC demand recovery and AI server ramp-up. We model AI servers to 
contribute more in 2026-28E. We see P/E valuation as fair at the current level compared 
to its peers and already reflects the solid growth in 2026-28E. Catalysts for further 
upside/downside would be its customers’ demand on AI servers and general server 
demand outlook.

Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$56 is based on 14.2x 2027E P/E, which is 
the company’s average P/E for the past 10 years. 
Key upside/downside risks: (1) stronger-/weaker-than-expected PC market recovery; 
(2) faster-/slower-than-expected AI server ramp-up; and (3) 
stronger-/weaker-than-expected demand for general servers.

Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

Feedback:  Read across from Nan

our coverage
Substrate CCL: Nan Ya New Material (not covered) has started shipping BT
substrate CCL in small volumes, primarily to domestic customers. We believe this is

driven by raw material supply constraints and has positive implications to non-
Japanese substrate CCL suppliers, including Elite Material, as customers diversify
away from incumbent suppliers.
Glass cloth: Nan Ya New Material expects the price trend of 7628-type glass cloth
to normalize from 2Q27 onwards, while thin/super-thin E glass cloth  remains very
under-supplied in CY27, which should provide support to thin E glass cloth pricing
through next year. The implication to Kingboard is therefore mixed.

Copper foil: Nan Ya New Material sees HTE copper foil declining while its demand
for advanced RTF and HVLP copper foils continues to surge, which bodes well for
Co-Tech, which has exited HTE copper foil business to focus on advanced RTF and
HVLP. The company also sources high-end HVLP from Co-Tech.
Customers: Nan Ya New Material mentioned major Chinese PCB vendors are all its
customers, including Shennan, WUS, Shengyi Elec, Founder, Kinwong, etc. For the

overseas market, it is also engaged with ISU, TTM and Unimicron, primarily for North
America-based customers.
Order change? Nan Ya New Material has not seen any server-related order cuts so
far; it noted the PCB/CCL  demand from the China compute market is growing
strongly. This also has positive implications to Chinese PCB/CCL suppliers Shennan
and Shengyi.

ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

證。非經本公司同意，不得將本報告加以引用或轉載

1 
3167 大量(Ta Liang)

報告日期：2026/11/12 研 究 員：翁浩軒 (Davy Wong)  
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 公司 TM4 背鑽機具技術領先優勢 
 成型機受惠光模塊建置需求放量成長 
 半導體及光通訊檢量測為新成長動能

一. 3Q26 毛利率優於預期，EPS 10.02 元 
大量 3Q26 營收 36.71 億(QoQ+28.4%、YoY+159.5%)，受惠於 PCB
對高階鑽孔機需求持續放量，營收創歷史新高，毛利率升至 50.17%
優於預期，主要因高階 PCB 機台及 TM3 出貨提升，稅後淨利 9.05
億(QoQ+66.62%、YoY+320.97%)，EPS 10.02 元。

二、AI 帶動高階 PCB 設備放量 
受惠於 AI 趨勢，鑽孔機及成型機需求均放量，大量之鑽孔機接單
提升，高階機型 2025 年出貨 501 台，至 2026 年 10 月底已出貨 1189
台，全年出貨近 1500 台，2027 年已確定訂單已約 600 台，仍有許
多仍在洽談中，而內層銅量測 2025 年為 TM2/TM3 系列，出貨 19
台，2026 年 TM2/TM3 出貨 51 台，TM4 已出貨 35 台，至年底仍有
10 台訂單待出，2027 年預估將全年轉為 TM4，因終端客戶向 PCB
廠指定用大量的 TM4，我們預估有望出貨近 100 台。成型機部分，
今年前三季營收比重 30%，今年出貨比預期高，主要因光模塊需
求提升，採 mSAP 製程需用 CCD 成型機，公司成型機品質及市占
率高，預估 2027 年成長性不小於鑽孔機。整體而言，公司 PCB 在
手訂單已上千台，2027 年鑽孔機及成型機預期仍有雙位數成長。

三. 2026 / 2027 年獲利創高，預估 EPS 31.34 元/ 65.01 元 
展望 2027 年，PCB 及半導體業務均大幅成長，AI 帶動高階 Server
板用量，PCB 鑽孔機、成型機需求強勁，高階 PCB 設備出貨維持
雙位數成長; 半導體業務方面，先進製程、先進封裝需求爆發，大
量跨入半導體領域營運逐步發酵，除了主流封裝外，CoWoS、
SoIC、FOPLP、CPO 產能擴增將帶動相關檢量測設備需求，預估大
量 2027 年半導體營收有望倍增。預估大量 2026 年營收 126.14 億
(YoY+148.41%)，毛利率 47.65%，稅後淨利 28.28 億(YoY+294.83%)，
EPS 31.34 元。預估 2027 年營收 221.39 億(YoY+75.71%)，毛利率
50.39%，稅後淨利 58.71 億(YoY+107.63%)，EPS 65.01 元。

四.投資建議：買進，目標價 1300 元 
大量為 PCB 鑽孔機、成型機大廠，為市場高階設備品質及技術領
導廠商，市占率亦高。過去設備出貨量隨景氣循環而波動明顯，
近年受惠於 AI 對高階 PCB 設備需求放量，加上切入半導體領域量
逐步發酵，公司檢量測設備於先進封裝之滲透率提升，加上 CPO
設備亦準備出貨，公司營運正向。考量到 1)高階 PCB 設備接單暢
旺、2)2027 年獲利翻倍成長、3)公司跨入先進封裝及 CPO 領域評
價有望提升，參考 2027 年 EPS 給予 20 倍本益比，目標價 1300 元，
建議買進。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
大量科技股份有限公司成立於 1980 年，總部位於桃園，公司以自
行研發製造控制器為競爭核心技術，發展鑽孔機、成型機等設備，
控制器可作為降低成型機振動及提升鑽孔精準度。公司業務含概
PCB 及半導體，PCB 設備產品包含 PCB 鑽孔定位、PCB 成型切割、
點膠、曲線滾塗等塗佈製程電漿之蝕刻、鍍膜等表面改質製程等；
半導體設備事業已於 2026 年 8 月 1 日分拆為大量先進智能，目前
由大量 100%持有，產品涵蓋 AOI、量測及自動化設備。公司之生
產工廠包括桃園八德廠、大陸南京廠、蘇州漣水廠，客戶含蓋兩
岸 PCB 廠及台灣 Foundry 及 OSAT 廠，包括欣興、健鼎、瀚宇博，
中國 PCB 廠深南、景旺、超聲等，台灣競爭對手包括台灣恩得、
東台、德國 SCHMOLL、中國大族激光等。

3 
二.產業趨勢概況 
1. 大量以內層銅深度量測建立背鑽技術門檻 
AI 算力需求對於資料傳輸要求提高，PCB 訊號傳輸加快下背鑽需
求提升，加上 PCB 層數增加對於背鑽精度要求亦提升。PCB 鑽孔
製程是經由通孔->填孔(通孔鍍銅)->背鑽，PCB 鑽孔將特定層數導
通後需要將多餘的銅柱鑽除，以降低訊號干擾，隨著 PCB 有效頻
寬從 Blackwell 28GHz 提升至 Rubin 56GHz，背鑽精度對殘銅要求
從±3mil(殘銅共振頻率約 45GHz)提升至±2mil(殘銅共振頻率
60GHz)，在背鑽機精度要求提高趨勢下，大量自製 TM4 量測機
可進行孔內深度量測，精準辨識孔內銅層/絕緣層實際位置，先建
立 3D 孔洞深度地圖後再進行背鑽，因此 TM4 量測機搭配大量自
製控制器與軟體的背鑽機成為高階 AI Server 背鑽的主流選擇，因
為 TM4 只與自有背鑽機整合銷售，不外搭外部機台，公司因此建
立背鑽技術門檻。

2. AI 帶動高階 PCB 設備放量 
大量主要產品以 PCB 設備為主，營收比重約 9 成，三大產品線包
含 PCB 鑽孔機、PCB 成型機及塗膠設備。鑽孔機涵蓋一般型與高
階型，差異在於是否有 CCD，另提背鑽量測設備(Stub Mapper) TM
系列，能將孔位精準量測，規劃背鑽深度地圖，精度可達正負 2mil; 
成型機涵蓋一般型與 CCD 高階成型機; 塗膠設備主要應用於
AI/AR 眼鏡邊緣及玻璃基板邊緣塗膠保護。鑽孔機部分，今年前三
季營收比重 62%，受惠於 AI 趨勢，Server 板均需要背鑽，大量之
鑽孔機接單提升，高階機型 2025 年出貨 501 台，至 2026 年 10 月
底已出貨 1189 台，全年出貨近 1500 台，2027 年已確定訂單已約
600 台，仍有許多仍在洽談中，而內層銅量測 2025 年為 TM2/TM3
系列，出貨 19 台，2026 年 TM2/TM3 出貨 51 台，TM4 已出貨 35
台，至年底仍有 10 台訂單待出，2027 年預估將全年轉為 TM4，因
終端客戶向 PCB 廠指定用大量的 TM4，我們預估有望出貨近 100
台。TM2/TM3 是單軸架構，適用前期樣品打樣；打樣獲得終端認
可、要進入批量量產時，必須採用四軸的 TM4 才能符合產能與
精度效率。成型機部分，今年前三季營收比重 30%，今年出貨比
預期高，主要因光模塊需求提升，採 mSAP 製程，需用 CCD 成型
機，公司成型機品質及市占率都高，預估 2027 年成長性不小於鑽
孔機。整體而言，公司 PCB 在手訂單台數已上千台，2027 年鑽孔
機及成型機預期仍有雙位數成長。

圖二、大量高階機種出貨

資料來源：大量

3. 半導體檢量測設備為新成長動能 
大量近年切入半導體領域，以大量先進智能(TLSmart)品牌行銷，
公司四大核心能力包括機構設計、快速設備控制、高精度量測與 
AI 檢測視覺系統、系統整合能力，產品線含蓋半導體前中後段，
主要提供 Metrology（量測）、AOI 檢測、自動化設備，公司在先
進封裝技術節點角色，包括 CoWoS：提供 CoW 及 oS 端各關鍵
站點的量測與檢驗設備; 3DIC / SoIC：提供三大量測系統（CMP Pad 
Metrology、Wafer Trim Gap Metrology、Wafer Bump Shift Edge Profile 
Metrology）; 面板級封裝（FOPLP / CoPoS）：因應 Chip 尺寸擴
大中介層需求轉向 Panel，推出支援 310×310mm、600×600mm、510
×515mm 之量測檢測設備，多數已達量產或小批量階段; 共同光學
封裝（CPO / 矽光子）：與美系國際 Tier 1 大廠共同開發 CPO OE 
與 Package 前處理系統、光學檢查機及後製程一體整合機，2026
年建第一條試產線，2027 下半年預估量產。半導體整體 BB 
Ratio>2，預估今年第四季出貨開始放量增加。

4. 今明年產能持續擴充 
公司目前自有產能已可達到 400 台/月，明年規劃提升至 500 台/
月以上，目前設備組裝時間方面，一般機型組裝約在 1 週以內，
高階機型、TM4 則約需 1.5 倍時間。公司除了自有工廠產能外，
亦接洽多家外包，2027 年如果外包認證順利產能有望達 600 台/月。

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

新高，毛利率升至 50.17%優於預期，主要因高階 PCB 機台及 TM3
出貨提升，稅後淨利 9.05 億(QoQ+66.62%、YoY+320.97%)，EPS 10.02
元。

2、2026 / 2027 年獲利創高，預估 EPS 31.34 元/ 65.01 元

稅後淨利 28.28 億(YoY+294.83%)，EPS 31.34 元。預估 2027 年營收
221.39 億 (YoY+75.71%) ， 毛 利 率 50.39% ， 稅 後 淨 利 58.71 億
(YoY+107.63%)，EPS 65.01 元。

或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保

1 
3597 映興(AVIN)

報告日期：2026/11/17 研 究 員：洪瑞陽 (Ryan Hung) 
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 資料中心新專案自 3Q26 開始放量，3Q26 營收 3.26 億元，QoQ 
+28.8%、YoY +39.6%，稅後 EPS 0.55 元；10 月營收進一步
提高至 1.30 億元，MoM +5.03%、YoY +76.69%，本研究預估
4Q26 營收 3.97 億元，下半年營運動能明顯高於上半年。 
 2027 年新專案進入完整年度貢獻，本研究預估台達電集團訂單
營收占比由 2025 年的 41.5%提升至 2026 年約 50%、2027 年
約 65%，成為未來兩年主要營收增量來源。 
 受惠新訂單放量，本研究預 2026／2027 年營收分別為 11.8 億
元／17.8 億元，2027 年 YOY+50.8%，新增業務以台達電相關
OEM 線束為主，毛利率將維持一定區間較難提升，預估 2026
／2027 年毛利率 23.9%／23.4%，獲利成長主要來自營收放
量，稅後 EPS 分別為 1.61／3.31 元。

一、2H26 新專案開始放量，營收動能明顯轉強 
3Q26 營收 3.26 億元，QoQ +28.8%、YoY +39.6%，毛利率 24.43%，
較 2Q26 維持前期水準；營業利益 2,993 萬元，營業利益率 9.17%，
較 2Q26 的 7.43%提高 1.74 個百分點，稅後淨利 2,156 萬元，EPS 
0.55 元。累計前三季營收 7.78 億元、EPS 0.90 元，營運較 2025
年同期明顯改善。

受惠資料中心新專案持續放量，7／8／9 月營收分別為 9,788 萬元
／1.05 億元／1.24 億元，呈現逐月成長。10 月營收進一步提升至
1.30 億元，MoM +5.03%、YoY +76.69%，創單月新高。本研究依
10 月實際營收、既有業務營運水準及新專案出貨進度推估，4Q26
營收約 3.97 億元，QoQ +21.8% ，毛利率 23.65%，營業利益率
11.1%，稅後 EPS 約 0.71 元，下半年營運成長動能持續增強。

二、AI 電力需求成長，新專案放量，2027 年全年貢獻 
AI 算力需求提升帶動資料中心電力密度增加，電源架構朝高功
率、高電流及高可靠度方向發展，HVDC、PDU、UPS／BBU 及
PSU 等電源與備援系統需求同步增加。映興產品已涵蓋 AI 資料中
心機櫃、備援電力模組、基地台機房及電源供應系統等線束模組，
公司產品亦可延伸至大電流與高可靠度電力傳輸應用。

映興在 AI 資料中心的供應模式以電源系統相關線束及連接元件為
投資評等：買進 
目標價位：59 元 
      18X 2027 EPS 
參考價位：38.5 元 
     11.6X 2027 EPS

2 
主，並非直接供應 CSP，因此營運成長主要隨下游客戶資料中心
電源系統出貨放大。本研究預期 2026 年新專案僅貢獻部分年度，
2027 年除具全年化效果外，出貨仍持續 Ramp，因此預估新專案
相關營收較 2026 年成長逾 2 倍，帶動台達電集團訂單營收占比由
2025 年的約四成提高至 2026 年約 50%、2027 年約 65%，成為未
來兩年主要營收增量來源。

三、既有應用維持成長，較高毛利產品支撐產品組合 
除 AI 資料中心外，公司既有產品橫跨工業應用、移動載具、車載
電子及儲能等領域。移動載具涵蓋無人機、機器狗、自行車、摩
托車、滑板車及船外機等所需的防水連接器與線束模組；車載電
子則供應車用風扇、OBC 及煞車系統線束；工業應用包括氫燃料
電池、馬達、機械手臂與家電相關線束及代理元件。

公司自有防水連接器累積 93 項國內外專利，具微型大電流、高插
拔次數、IP68 防水及抗火花等特性；OEM 線束則可透過自主運算
工具進行阻抗與結構優化設計，並取得 ISO 9001、ISO 14001、IATF 
16949 及 UL 等認證。

本研究預期車載及移動載具等較高毛利產品持續成長，可部分抵
銷 AI 資料中心新增 OEM 線束占比提高造成的毛利率負向影響。
3Q26 實際毛利率仍達 24.43%，顯示新增訂單放量尚未造成毛利率
明顯下降；惟隨 2027 年 OEM 營收占比進一步提高，產品組合仍
有毛利率些許負向影響。因此映興未來兩年營運型態將呈現「營
收快速成長、毛利率大致維持、費用率下降」，獲利成長主要來
自營收規模放大，而非毛利率擴張。

四、預估 2026／2027 年稅後 EPS 1.61／3.31 元 
2026 年受惠資料中心新專案自 2H26 開始貢獻，本研究預估全年
營收 11.75 億元，YoY +36.5% ；毛利率 23.9%、營業費用約 1.82
億元，費用率由 2025 年的 17.4%降至 15.5%，帶動營業利益約 9,837
萬元、營業利益率提升至 8.37%，EPS 1.61 元。

2027 年新專案進入完整年度貢獻，本研究預估全年營收 17.75 億
元，YoY +51.1%；毛利率 23.4%、營業費用約 2.20 億元，費用率
進一步降至 12.4%，營業利益約 1.95 億元、營業利益率 11.0%。
預估稅後淨利約 1.31 億元，EPS 3.31 元，YoY 約+106%。 
五、投資建議：買進，目標價 59 元 
隨台達電訂單新專案貢獻，映興有新的營運動能，帶動營收與 EPS
正向成長，以 2027 年 EPS3.31 計算，根據歷史區間與同業評價，
給予 18 倍本益比，目標價 59 元

3 
貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況 
一、公司簡介 
映興電子成立於 1986 年，主要從事連接線束模組、代理元件及自
有防水連接器，營運據點涵蓋台灣、中國深圳與昆山、泰國及美
國。公司產品可分為三類：第一為 OEM／ODM／OBM 連接線束
模組；第二為代理元件，包括 Sensata 溫度保護元件、日本 Kurabe
特殊電線及 Anderson Power 大電流連接器；第三為自有防水連接
器。公司目前聚焦 AI 伺服器、工業應用、移動載具、車載電子及
綠能儲能五大應用市場。

公司核心能力在於複雜線束加工、防水連接器設計及代理元件整
合。自有防水連接器累積 93 項國內外專利，具備 IP68 防水、抗
火花、高插拔次數及微型大電流等特性；OEM 線束則可透過自主
運算工具進行阻抗與結構優化設計，並取得 ISO 9001、ISO 14001、
IATF 16949 及 UL 等認證。相較一般單純線材加工廠，公司可利
用一線品牌代理權作為客戶導入入口，再延伸至線束加工與自有
連接器，提高單一客戶合作深度。

五大應用中，AI 伺服器主要供應資料中心 HVDC／PDU／CDU 電
源機櫃、UPS／BBU 備援電池櫃、TPS 基地台機房及 PSU 內部線
束；工業應用涵蓋氫燃料電池、馬達、機械手臂及家電所需線束
與代理元件；移動載具包括無人機、機器狗、自行車、摩托車、
滑板車及船外機所需防水連接器與線束；車載電子則供應車用風
扇、OBC 及煞車系統線束；綠能儲能則以太陽能儲能、大電流連
接器及電池櫃線束為主，相關技術亦可延伸至 AI 資料中心 BBU
備援電力應用。

資料來源：公司官網

二、AI 新專案放量，台達電集團訂單營收占比持續提升 
AI 資料中心為映興目前最重要的新增成長來源。隨 AI 算力需求
提升，資料中心電力系統逐步朝高功率、高電流與高可靠度方向
發展，HVDC、PDU、UPS／BBU 及 PSU 等電源與備援系統需求
同步增加。公司目前相關產品已涵蓋 AI 資料中心機櫃、備援電力
模組、基地台機房及電源供應系統等線束模組，因此可隨下游資
料中心電源系統需求擴張而受惠。公司亦已具備 UPS 電池系統、
IT Rack 、PDU 及電池模組串接等產品經驗，可延伸至 AI 資料中
心大電流及高可靠度電力傳輸應用。

公司並非直接供應 CSP，而是透過台達電集團訂單切入終端雲端
服務業者供應鏈。映興供應內容集中於資料中心電源系統內部線
束與連接元件，因此營運節奏與台達電集團資料中心電源業務高
度連動。除了資料中心電源機櫃外，公司在台達電集團既有業務
亦涵蓋 PSU、車載充電系統、基地台機房、儲能櫃及自動化設備
等產品，客戶合作範圍並非僅限於 AI 資料中心單一應用。

本研究估算，2025 年台達電集團訂單營收占比約四成；2026 年上
半年新專案尚未正式進入主要營收貢獻期，因此相關營收仍以
TPS、車用及既有電源產品為主。隨資料中心新專案於 3Q26 開始
放量，7、8、9 月公司合併營收逐月提升，3Q26 營收達 3.26 億元，
QoQ +28.8%。10 月營收進一步增加至 1.30 億元，MoM +5.03%、
YoY +76.69%，本研究預期台達電集團訂單相關營收成長動能自下
半年起明顯轉強。

本研究對資料中心新專案採 Bottom-up 推估，將台達電集團訂單
營收區分為既有業務與資料中心新增業務，並依已公告營收、既
有業務正常營運水準及後續專案 Ramp 節奏進行推估，而非直接
以客戶占比反推公司營收。由於 2026 年新專案僅貢獻部分年度，

5 
2027 年同時享有全年化與出貨增加兩項效果，本研究預估相關新
專案營收較 2026 年成長逾 2 倍，帶動台達電集團訂單營收占比由
2026 年約 50%進一步提高至 2027 年約 65%，為未來兩年最主要
營收增量來源。

惟台達電集團訂單相關業務主要仍以 OEM 線束為主，毛利率相對
低於自有防水連接器、車載及部分移動載具產品，因此台達電集
團訂單營收占比快速提升將對產品組合形成一定毛利壓力。不
過，3Q26 毛利率仍達 24.43%，維持前期水準，加上集團既有產能
可因應新增訂單，營收規模放大有助於固定製造成本攤提。本研
究預估 2026／2027 年毛利率約 23.9%／23.4%，營業利益率則由
8.3%提高至 11.0%，顯示本輪成長主要來自 AI 帶動營收放量及費
用率下降，而非毛利率成長。 
三、車載與移動載具高毛利產品及其他業務正向發展 
移動載具產品涵蓋無人機、機器狗、自行車、摩托車、滑板車、
輪椅及船外機所需的防水連接器與線束模組。公司在此領域主要
競爭力來自自有防水連接器，可提供 IP68、防水、抗火花及高插
拔次數設計，適合戶外、震動及高濕度等環境，產品附加價值高
於一般 OEM 線束。公司原有兩輪載具為既有核心市場，近年亦逐
步向無人機與四足機器人等新應用延伸。

考量自行車庫存逐步正常化、無人機非紅供應鏈需求提高，以及
機器狗等新型移動載具應用擴張，本研究預估移動載具中短期營
收可維持中個位數成長，並以約 5～8%作為 2027 年相關業務成長
假設。由於此類產品較多採用公司自有防水連接器及客製化線
束，其毛利結構優於一般 OEM 產品，因此除營收成長外，亦可作
為未來產品組合的重要支撐。

車載電子方面，公司主要供應車用風扇、On-board Charger 及煞車
系統線束，已取得 IATF 16949 車用認證，可切入對品質、可靠度
及認證要求較高的汽車供應鏈。公司現有產品已涵蓋車載充電、
煞車系統及相關電力電子應用，隨全球汽車朝電動化與智慧化發
展，電力電子、充電系統及熱管理需求仍具中長期成長趨勢。

考量車用產品認證週期較長、導入後客戶黏著度相對較高，加上
電動車及高階車用電子滲透率持續提升，本研究預估車載電子中
長期營收成長約 10～15%。部分高階車用產品專案能見度亦延伸
至未來數年，預期車載業務可持續成為公司較穩定、且毛利率優
於一般 OEM 線束的產品線。

工業應用為公司既有營收的重要來源，產品涵蓋氫燃料電池、馬

達、機械手臂、燃油引擎所需線束模組與代理元件，以及空調壓
縮機、吹風機、吸塵器等家電使用的代理元件；客戶應用橫跨燃
料電池、工業自動化與消費型家電。

本研究預期工業應用仍受惠工業自動化、燃料電池及資料中心相
關電力需求，維持穩健成長。不過須注意公司應用別與客戶別分
類並非完全對應，部分 AI 資料中心及基礎建設相關產品亦可能歸
類於工業應用，因此本研究在財測時不直接套用單一工業應用成
長率，以避免與台達電集團訂單 AI 專案重複計算。

綠能儲能方面，公司產品包括太陽能儲能系統、大電流儲能連接
器及電池櫃串接線束，其技術與 AI 資料中心 BBU 在大電流傳輸、
電池模組串接及高可靠度連接等方面具有共通性。隨儲能與資料
中心備援電力需求增加，公司過去累積的電池櫃及大電流連接技
術可同時支援傳統儲能及 AI 資料中心相關應用，提供中長期新增
業務機會。

整體而言，本研究預期其他客戶業務將由車載、移動載具、工業
及儲能等既有應用共同支撐。2026 年在既有應用回升下，預估其
他客戶營收約 5.86 億元，YoY 約+16.5%；考量 2027 年基期提高，
且工業分類中部分 AI 營收已另於台達電集團訂單模型估算，本研
究採較保守成長假設，預估 2027 年其他客戶營收約 6.27 億元，
YoY 約+7%。此部分雖非未來兩年最大營收增量來源，但較高毛
利產品比重有助於抵銷 AI OEM 線束占比上升造成的產品組合壓
力。 
叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估 
一、預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 1.61 元 
本研究將營收拆分為「台達電集團訂單」與「其他客戶」兩部分。
台達電集團訂單再區分既有業務及資料中心新專案；其他客戶則
依車載、移動載具、工業及儲能等既有應用的實際營運水準與成
長趨勢估算。台達電集團訂單營收占比為兩部分個別預估後除算
得出，而非預先假設占比後回推營收。

3Q26 營收 3.26 億元，QoQ +28.8%、YoY +39.6%，較本研究原預
估 3.22 億元高約 1.5%；毛利率 24.43%，優於原預估 23.2%，惟營
業費用 4,976 萬元，亦高於原預估 4,200 萬元，營業利益 2,993 萬
元、營業利益率 9.17%，稅後 EPS 0.55 元。雖然營收與毛利率優
於原預估，但費用同步增加，顯示新增訂單放量後仍需考量營運
規模擴大所帶來的費用成長。

10 月營收 1.30 億元，MoM +5.03%、YoY +76.69%，亦高於本研究

原估約 1.23 億元。考量資料中心新專案出貨進度優於原先預期，
本研究上修 4Q26 營收至 3.97 億元，QoQ +21.8%。毛利率考量 3Q26
實際維持 24%以上，且產能利用率提高有助於固定製造成本攤
提，預估 4Q26 毛利率 24.0%；營業費用則依 3Q26 實際水準估約
5,000 萬元，營業利益約 4,534 萬元、營業利益率 11.4%，稅 後 EPS
約 0.73 元。下半年營收合計約 7.24 億元，較上半年增加約 60.3%。

本研究預估 2026 全年營收 11.75 億元，YoY +36.5%，其中台達電
集團訂單營收約 5.89 億元，占營收比重約 50%；其他客戶營收約
5.86 億元，YoY 約+16.5%，維持雙位數成長。

雖新增台達電集團訂單以 OEM 線束為主，產品組合對毛利率形成
一定的負向影響，但 3Q26 實際毛利率仍維持 24.43%，反映營收
規模增加可部分抵銷產品組合影響。本研究預估 2026 年毛利率約
23.9%，較 2025 年的 23.6%小幅提升。營業費用預估約 1.82 億元，
YoY 約+21.9%，增幅低於營收，費用率由 2025 年的 17.4%下降至
15.5%，帶動營業利益約 1.00 億元、營業利益率提升至 8.37%。

業外損益預估約-598 萬元，考量前三季實際稅率及 4Q26 有效稅率
35%的假設，預估全年有效稅率約 31.7%，稅後淨利約 6,404 萬元，
EPS 1.63 元。獲利成長主要來自營收放量、固定成本攤提改善及
營業費用率下降，而非單純依賴毛利率提升。

二、預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 3.31 元  
2027 年營收成長主要來自資料中心新專案。由於 2026 年新專案僅
自下半年開始貢獻，2027 年除完整貢獻全年外，出貨仍持續 Ramp 
up。本研究考量 3Q26 實際營收與 10 月營收均優，預估新專案相
關營收較 2026 年成長逾 2 倍；既有台達電集團訂單業務則維持穩
定，預估全年台達電集團訂單營收約 11.48 億元。

其他客戶方面，本研究預估 2026 年營收約 5.86 億元，2027 年考
量車載、移動載具及其他既有業務持續成長，但 2026 已有較高基
期，因此採較正常化成長假設，預估全年營收約 6.27 億元，YoY
約+7%。

兩者加總後，預估 2027 年營收 17.75 億元，YoY +51.1%，台達電
集團訂單營收占比提高至約 65%。相較原預估 16.94 億元，本次主
要反映資料中心新專案出貨進度提前，而非 ASP 上升。

季度營收預估呈逐季上升，1Q27／2Q27／3Q27／4Q27 分別為 4.05
／4.29／4.58／4.83 億元。新專案出貨增加使台達電集團訂單營收
占比逐步提高，但考量 OEM 比重上升，毛利率維持；另一方面，

三、台達電集團訂單為主要成長，其他業務維持穩健  
台達電集團訂單方面，本研究預估既有業務維持正常營運水準，
資料中心新專案則自 2H26 進入放量階段。考量 3Q26 實際營收及
10 月營收均優，預估 2026／2027 年台達電集團訂單營收分別為
5.89／11.48 億元，YoY 約+65%／+95%，占合併營收比重由 2026
年的約 50%提高至 2027 年約 65%，為未來兩年最主要營收增量來
源。

其他客戶方面，本研究依公司既有應用營運趨勢進行估算，預估
2026 年營收約 5.86 億元，2027 年增加至約 6.27 億元，YoY 約+7%。
2027 年增速較 2026 正常化，主要考量 2026 已有較高基期。

公司簡介 
貿聯-KY 是全球領先的連接器公司之一：貿
聯-KY 總部設於加州 Fremont，3Q26 產品
比重為資訊科技與數據傳輸 48％、工業用
31％、車用 8％、電器 12％；若以事業別
區分，HPC 占 46％、半導體設備(SPE)占

主要客戶： 
主要競爭對手：

張予柔 yurou.chang@sinopac.com 
本次報告：法說會

貿聯-KY (3665 TT) 
併購綜效超預期，獲利動能再加速

永豐觀點 
併購 Interplex、新富生貢獻雙雙超預期，獲利上修循環啟動。 
 投資評價與建議 
重申買進建議：研究部本次上修貿聯財務預估：(1) Interplex 資料通訊業務
(Datacom Business)營收規模大幅超標：10 月首個完整併表月營收約新台幣 21
億元，大幅優於研究部原先預期之約 13 億元，研究部同步上修其 2026~2028 年
營收與獲利貢獻預估。(2) 新富生月營收規模自年初以來已成長逾一倍，憑藉資料
中心高階光纖跳線的製造與品質優勢，持續拓展新客戶，研究部預估其 2027 年營
收可持續倍增。(3) 產品組合轉佳、營運槓桿持續發揮：3Q26 毛利率季增 1.8 個
百點至 32.53%，且營業費用率降至約 12%，帶動營益率創 20.78%新高。綜合預
估貿聯 2026-2028 年集團營收分別為 1,074、1,659、2,161 億元(+51%、+55%、
+30%YoY)；稅後淨利 150、267、356 元(+66%、+79%、+33%YoY)，完全稀
釋後 EPS 分別為 75.25、134.07 及 178.24 元。考量 AI 供應鏈遇短期逆風，影響
評價表現，研究部將本益比調整為 18 倍，維持買進建議，目標價 2,810 元(2027
年及 2028 年之平均預估 EPS 156.16*18XPER)。

ESG 評析 
貿聯企業永續評鑑整體為 B+等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 B 等與 A 等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

### Page 2

2

貿聯-KY 是全球領先的連接器公司之一：貿聯-KY 是全球領先的連接器公司之一：
貿聯-KY 總部設於加州 Fremont，3Q26 產品比重為資訊科技與數據傳輸 48％、
工業用 31％、車用 8％、電器 12％；若以事業別區分，HPC 占 46％、半導體設
備(SPE)占 13％、其他事業占 41％。貿聯主要核心能力，在於高技術及客製化開
發零組件製造，產品開發以多角化及差異化策略，2026/1 完成收購新富生光電
(XFS)，2026/9/23 完成收購 Interplex 集團資料通訊業務(Interplex Datacom)，
產品線由電源與數據連接延伸至光纖跳線、機殼及機櫃等精密機構件，朝 AI 機櫃
次系統解決方案供應商發展。 
3Q26 財報公布，優於市場預期，營收與獲利同創新高，營益率首度突破 20%：
3Q26 營收 278.75 億元(+19.7% QoQ、+51.4% YoY)，毛利率 32.53%，季增 1.8
個百分點；營業利益率 20.78%，季增 3.6 個百分點、年增 1.2 個百分點，為公司
首度突破 20%，獲利表現優於市場預期。成長主要來自 HPC，3Q26 HPC 營收季
增 30%、年增 61%，營收比重由 2Q26 的 42%回升至 46%，其中電源產品為成
長主力，帶動產品組合改善；半導體設備營收亦季增 11%、年增 70%，各事業全
面成長。費用方面，營收規模擴大但營業費用未等比例增加，營業費用率降至約
12%的歷史低點，顯示獲利率改善來自規模效益與費用效率，而非一次性因素，具
備延續性。稅後淨利 42.93 億元(+44.0% QoQ、+63.3% YoY)，稀釋後 EPS 21.51
元，營收、淨利與 EPS 皆創歷史新高；前三季累計 EPS 48.04 元，已超越 2025 全
年之 46.07 元。

營運現況與分析

### Page 3

3

研究部上修 2026~2028 年營收獲利預估，原因有三： 
1. Interplex 資料通訊業務(Datacom Business)營收提前兩年達標：Interplex
的資料通訊業務(Datacom Business)自 9/23 起併表，3Q26 僅貢獻 7 天營
收；10 月為首個完整併表月，帶動貿聯 10 月營收達 118.46 億元，月增 26.9%、
年增 80.0%，其中 Interplex 貢獻 6,700 萬美元(約新台幣 21 億元)。對照公司
宣布併購時揭露之年營收 3.92 億美元(月平均約 3,300 萬美元)，Interplex 月
營收已倍增，大幅優於市場預期。研究部看好併購綜效將逐步顯現：Interplex
主要供應 AI 伺服器機殼、機櫃等精密機構件，研究部認為其營收較併購時大
幅成長，主因 AI 機櫃出貨量較年初明顯放大，且併入貿聯後，Interplex 可運
用集團的資金、客戶關係與製造資源擴大接單，雙方產品整合後可提供涵蓋配
電、數據連接、機構件與液冷的機櫃次系統方案，單一 AI 機櫃之可供應產值
明顯擴大。研究部本次上修 2027、2028 年 Interplex Datacom Business 為
貿聯帶來的營收預估，分別為 268、308 億元(原預估 183、250 億元)。 
2. 新富生光通訊規模快速放大，營收占比持續提升：新富生(XFS) 專精於光纖互
連產品的設計與製造，主要產品包括單芯／雙芯跳線、光纖尾纖、多芯光纖以
及 MTP/MPO 高密度連接器，月營收規模自 2026 年初以來成長逾一倍，獲
利能力同步改善，展現規模化高精密光學互連業務的效益。資料中心對光纖組
件的品質與一致性要求遠高於一般應用，新富生在高階光纖跳線的製造經驗與
品質優勢，加上貿聯集團的客戶網絡與全球產能布局，有助其持續拓展新客戶。
隨 AI 資料中心機櫃間光纖連接需求成長，研究部預估新富生 2027 年營收可
望再翻倍增長，且其獲利率改善亦有助提升貿聯整體獲利結構。 
3. 3Q26 營益率創新高，營運槓桿持續發揮：3Q26 營業費用率約 12%，較 1H26
的 13.7%明顯下降，顯示貿聯在承接更大規模、更複雜的客戶專案時，製造、
工程與行政職能的效率同步提升，費用無須等比例增加。4Q26 起 Interplex
完整併表，營收規模再擴大，研究部預期營益率改善幅度可望大於毛利率，稅
後淨利成長幅度將高於營收。 
HPC 電源成長最強，新平台升級為下一波動能：3Q26 HPC 營收年增 61%、季增
30%，前三季累計年增 77%。隨運算功耗提升，電源產品切入規格更高的新一代
平台；數據連接方面，貿聯同時布局銅纜與光纖，因應不同傳輸距離與頻寬需求。
貿聯於 10 月 OCP Global Summit 2026 展出適用於 OCP 及 NVIDIA MGX 機櫃
之 800V/400VDC 高功率電源方案，以及 1.6T 液冷交換器 OSFP 連接器、機櫃管
理控制與漏液偵測系統，產品範圍由電源線材延伸至配電、液冷與監控。研究部認
為，隨 Vera Rubin 平台放量及 HVDC 架構導入，單一機櫃的電源產品規格與產值
將持續提升，為 2027 年 HPC 成長主軸。 
次世代光通訊布局：貿聯 2026 年先後於 OFC 展示 Optical Shuffle、於 Computex
展示 CPO 交換器托盤方案；9 月於 ECOC 2026 展示與 ams OSRAM 合作之
microVCSEL 光學互連方案，採用 850nm 二維可定址 microVCSEL 陣列，以多通
道、低速率(wide-and-slow)架構降低功耗與延遲，貿聯負責光纖陣列組裝、光學
封裝、光學測試與量產製造；10 月於 OCP Global Summit 2026 展出 CPO、
800G/1.6T 及 XPO 光收發模組、光纖陣列(FAU)與多芯光纖連接等方案。公司表
示產業未來可能並行採用多種光學架構，貿聯以製造與整合能力參與各技術路線，
不需押注單一方案。 
半導體設備成長幅度為產業兩倍以上：3Q26 半導體設備(SPE)營收年增 70%、季
增 11%，營收占比由 3Q25 的 11%提升至 13%，前三季累計年增 48%。根據國
際半導體產業協會（SEMI）於 2026 年 7 月公布之預測，全球前段晶圓製造設備

### Page 4

4

(WFE)市場 2026 年預估年增 23.1%至 1,439 億美元，貿聯 SPE 成長幅度約為產業
的兩倍；自 2023 年以來貿聯之 SPE 業務成長即大幅超越整體 WFE 市場，超額成
長主因貿聯在客戶端的角色由供應線材零組件，擴大至高階組裝、模組與次系統製
造、測試及系統整合，應用以薄膜沉積與蝕刻設備為主，微影與檢測設備為輔；隨
設備製程日益複雜，客戶傾向將更多製造工作委外，以集中資源於核心技術，貿聯
單一設備平台之內含價值隨之提升。SEMI 預估 WFE 市場 2027 年及 2028 年將分
別成長 21.8%及 14.1%，2028 年規模可望突破 2,000 億美元，主要由先進邏輯、
HBM 等先進記憶體投資帶動；公司亦表示，許多客戶對設備投資的強勁預期延伸
至 2030 年以後，且客戶正主動協助貿聯擴充產能，以支援其自身長期成長。貿聯
在 SPE 領域的投資以擴充製造產能為主，Interplex 的精密機械加工與次系統整合
能力亦可與 SPE 業務互補，為 HPC 以外的第二成長引擎。 
隨伺服器架構升級驅動產值倍增，2027~2028 年獲利成長路徑清晰，重申買進： 
研究部認為，貿聯本次財報的關鍵不只是單季獲利優於預期，而是兩項併購同時優
於預期，帶動營收基期結構性墊高：10 月營收 118.46 億元，較 3Q26 月平均約
93 億元高出約 27%。在 AI 機櫃同時需要電源、數據傳輸(銅纜與光纖)與精密機構
件的趨勢下，貿聯透過 Interplex 與新富生補齊產品版圖，單一機櫃可供應產值持
續擴大；而未來一年內，Interplex 完整併表(4Q26)、新富生營收比重提升(2027)、
Vera Rubin 平台與 HVDC 電 源 放 量 (2027~2028) 及 次 世 代 光 互 連 產 品
(2H27~2028)等成長催化劑將陸續到位，獲利上修動能明確。 
評價方面，短期因市場擔憂 GPU 產品推出節奏可能放緩，台灣 AI 供應鏈評價短
期受到壓抑。研究部認為，CSP 資本支出與現行平台訂單短期不受影響，且貿聯的
客戶與產品布局相對分散：公司採取平台中立策略，產品同時供應 GPU 與 ASIC
架構之 AI 機櫃，並服務多家雲端服務業者；銅纜與光纖、電源與機構件等產品線
亦不依賴單一技術路線；此外，半導體設備與其他事業合計仍占營收逾五成，營運
受單一平台影響的程度低於純 GPU 供應鏈。惟考量主要客戶經營層異動可能影響
新平台推出節奏，研究部於評價上給予折價，本益比由前次之 25 倍調整為 18 倍。
搭配本次上修後之 EPS，維持買進建議，目標價 2,810 元(2027 年及 2028 年之平
均預估 EPS 156.16*18XPER)。

Document 330: European Economics Analyst_ UK Outlook 2027_ Cyclical Headwinds, Strengthening Fundamentals

Source PDF: all report pdf/European Economics Analyst_ UK Outlook 2027_ Cyclical Headwinds, Strengthening Fundamentals.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-11-15
Pages: 14
Status: ok

### Page 1

The UK economy proved surprisingly resilient in 2026. Growth accelerated n
despite rising energy prices and tighter financial conditions, and the labour 
market showed signs of stabilisation. The new government navigated a difficult 
fiscal backdrop to deliver a low-key budget that maintained the consolidation 
path. 
The coming quarters are likely to be more challenging. First, we expect growth to n
slow as the impact of the energy shock on consumer spending increases over the 
winter. Second, inflation will rise further as electricity and gas bills increase and 
the indirect effects of the energy shock build. We expect this to push the BoE to 
hike a second time, probably in February. Third, the government faces a difficult 
Spending Review; existing spending plans look too tight, but the Chancellor has 
limited fiscal headroom. 
Nonetheless, we see compelling reasons to be constructive on the economic n
outlook for 2027. First, some of the recent growth improvement looks structural, 
and the productivity trend is strengthening. We therefore expect activity to 
reaccelerate later in the year as some of the cyclical headwinds fade, and our 
Q4/Q4 growth forecast is above consensus at 1.4%. Second, we do not expect 
strong second-round effects from the energy shock into domestic inflation, so 
the BoE is likely to hike significantly less than priced. Third, we expect further 
fiscal consolidation, and a more favourable OBR forecast could ease the 
trade-offs facing the Chancellor. 
We also see scope for sentiment around the UK’s longer-term economic n
prospects to improve. The UK is well-placed to benefit from AI adoption given its 
strengths in professional services, and surveys indicate that companies expect a 
material boost to productivity growth in the coming years. The PM has also 
recently raised the possibility of closer EU ties, which could bring notable 
economic gains over time.

### Page 2

UK Outlook 2027: Cyclical Headwinds, Strengthening Fundamentals

The UK economy proved surprisingly resilient in 2026. Despite the energy shock keeping 
inflation elevated and causing the BoE to pivot from cutting to hiking, growth 
accelerated this year. The labour market showed signs of improvement, with 
unemployment falling and employment growth indicators ticking up. The new 
government navigated a challenging fiscal backdrop to deliver a low-key Budget that 
maintained the consolidation path.

Cyclical Headwinds 
The coming quarters are likely to prove more challenging in several respects. First, we 
expect growth to slow in the near term and run slightly below trend in Q4 and the first 
half of 2027. The impact of the energy shock on household finances is likely to build over 
the winter, with weak real disposable income growth resulting in a slowdown in 
consumer spending.

Exhibit 1: Growth Proved Surprisingly Resilient in 2026

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Bank of England
15 November 2026   2
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 3

Tighter financial conditions should also weigh on growth as mortgages are refixed at 
higher rates. Fiscal policy will likely turn more contractionary compared with this year 
given that current plans imply a larger consolidation in the primary balance in 2027.

While the labour market data improved over the last few months, we expect slower 
near-term growth to result in a modest further weakening. That is consistent with 
continued softness in most indicators of employment intentions. The pace of loosening 
should be gradual as the peak impact of last year’s labour cost increases on employment 
growth has probably passed. But we see the unemployment rate rising back to 5% by the 
middle of next year.

Exhibit 2: Consumer Spending Growth Likely to Slow

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics

Exhibit 3: Growth Headwinds from Rising Mortgage Rates and Tighter Fiscal Policy

Note that student loan figures for 2026-27 are affected by the accounting impact of a freeze in repayment thresholds, so the figures excluding student loans may 
give a better guide to the impacts on demand.

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Bloomberg, OBR
15 November 2026   3
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 4

Second, inflation is likely to increase further in the coming months. Consumer energy 
prices will rise notably in January on the back of higher electricity and gas bills. We also 
expect food and core goods inflation to accelerate as the indirect effects of the energy 
shock become more apparent. We also see renewed upward pressure in some regulated 
services prices that are indexed to headline inflation, and higher fuel costs will likely raise 
transport services prices. Overall, we expect inflation to peak at 4.3% in February and 
average 4.1% in 2027H1.

The peak in headline inflation will likely exceed the threshold at which the BoE has 
typically become more concerned about second-round effects. Taken together with 
hawkish recent commentary, that points to another 25bp BoE hike to 4.25%. This will 
probably come in February, although an earlier December move is also possible.

Exhibit 4: Renewed Labour Market Softness

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Bank of England

Exhibit 5: Rising Inflationary Pressures Point to a Second BoE Hike

The forecasts shown on the left are conditional on GS forecasts for energy prices.

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Bank of England
15 November 2026   4
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 5

Third, the government faces a difficult Spending Review. Existing plans imply a notable 
slowdown in departmental spending growth from next year onwards, which looks 
challenging to deliver given pressures on the health and defence budgets. History 
suggests that these plans will probably be notably upgraded. New policy priorities could 
require further outlays, with PM Burnham’s agenda likely to become clearer after the 
publication of his 10-Year Plan. But financing higher spending may be challenging given 
limited fiscal headroom, with revenues constrained by Labour’s manifesto not to raise 
taxes on working people. Welfare reforms could bring some savings over time but look 
likely to be accompanied by upfront investment in employment support.

Structural Improvement 
Despite these cyclical challenges, we see several reasons to be more constructive on the 
UK’s economic prospects for 2027. First, some of the tailwinds that supported growth 
this year look structural. Strength in business investment has partly reflected rising 
information and communications capex, which looks likely to be sustained by ongoing 
data centre investment. This investment should support continued growth in technology 
services output, where demand has increased on the back of AI adoption. Further 
growth in utilities capital spending linked to renewables should also continue to support 
demand.

Exhibit 6: A Challenging Spending Review

Source: OBR
15 November 2026   5
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 6

The productivity trend also appears to be improving, as growth in output per worker has 
increased significantly recently. Some of this may reflect the impact of last year’s 
employer National Insurance hike on hiring, but more recent data also show gains in 
sectors such as professional services that were less affected by labour cost increases. We 
also see more fundamental reasons to expect a pickup. While Brexit has probably 
weighed significantly on growth over the past decade, the worst of the headwind has 
likely passed. Survey data from the Decision Maker Panel indicate that early gains from 
AI adoption could also have modestly boosted the figures.

These factors, together with declines in energy prices, should mean that growth 
reaccelerates through the second half of next year. Overall, we expect a 1.4% Q4/Q4 
increase in GDP, above consensus expectations as well as the BoE and OBR forecasts.

Exhibit 7: Some Growth Drivers Look More Structural

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Yotzov et al. (2026)

Exhibit 8: Trend Productivity Growth is Probably Improving

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Various Academic Studies
15 November 2026   6
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 7

Second, we continue to expect limited second-round effects into domestic price 
pressures. Even after the recent decline in the unemployment rate, broader measures of 
utilisation (such as the vacancy/unemployment ratio) suggest that the labour market 
remains far looser than in 2022/23. That will likely reduce the extent to which higher 
headline inflation passes through into wage pressures. Indeed, early data indicate that 
pay settlements are likely to remain stable next year, and the BoE’s Decision Maker Panel 
survey shows little change in wage expectations since the start of the Middle East 
conflict. Private sector regular pay growth will probably accelerate from low levels but is 
likely to remain well below the levels seen after the previous energy shock.

With only a modest increase in pay growth, we expect overall labour cost inflation to be 
well below the levels seen in recent years. This is especially true in consumer-facing 
sectors, which saw sharper increases in wages over recent years and the largest impact 
from the employer National Insurance increases. More moderate labour cost inflation

Exhibit 9: Our GDP Forecast is Slightly Above Consensus

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Bloomberg, Bank of England, OBR

Exhibit 10: Second-Round Effects Likely to be Moderate

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Bank of England
15 November 2026   7
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 8

should help to keep a lid on underlying services inflation, despite cost pressures from 
higher energy and food prices.

Given moderate second-round effects, we think that the BoE will hike less than priced. 
After tightening policy in February, we expect the MPC to keep Bank Rate at 4.25% for 
the rest of the year, whereas the market implies two further rate increases. The risks 
around our baseline are skewed towards further tightening, but even on a 
probability-weighted basis our forecast is notably below current market pricing.

Third, 2027 is likely to see renewed progress on deficit reduction. The current fiscal 
plans imply a steep reduction in the primary deficit next year. While we think spending 
could overshoot the official projections, tax receipts could also come in somewhat 
higher. Further consolidation should mean that the UK continues to compare favourably 
to other advanced economies in terms of reducing borrowing. While the Spending 
Review does pose a challenge for the government, it is possible that changes in the

Exhibit 11: More Limited Labour Cost Increases Should Keep a Lid on Underlying Services Inflation

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, ONS

Exhibit 12: We Expect the BoE to Hike Less Than Priced

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Bloomberg
15 November 2026   8
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 9

economic forecast could help the Chancellor. The OBR’s wage growth forecast continues 
to look low, and an upgrade would boost the tax revenues projection, increasing fiscal 
space.

A Positive Long-Run Outlook 
We also see scope for sentiment around the UK’s longer-term economic prospects to 
improve. The UK is well-placed to benefit from AI adoption given its specialisation in 
high-value added services. IMF estimates indicate that a relatively high share of the 
workforce is exposed to AI, with a particularly large number of workers in roles that may 
see productivity gains but are less likely to be displaced. Survey data suggest that firms 
expect adoption to result in sizeable productivity increases in the coming years. Even 
though these gains are unlikely to be fully additive to the current productivity trend, the 
numbers still indicate upside risks to our estimates for supply growth.

Exhibit 13: Continued Fiscal Consolidation

On the left, the UK data are shown on a public sector basis.

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, OBR, Bank of England
15 November 2026   9
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 10

The UK is also well-positioned in several high growth innovative industries. This includes 
a world-leading position in life sciences, such as pharmaceuticals, biotechnology, 
medical technology, and genomics. The UK has a world-class research base, with the 
London/Oxford/Cambridge triangle a leading biotech cluster. 
Another possible source of long-run growth upside would be closer ties with the EU. The 
upcoming (November 20) summit could see some progress, with the two sides expected 
to finalise agrifood and emissions trading deals. But the Prime Minister has also 
indicated that he will use the summit to outline options for how the relationship might 
develop after the next election, potentially including a customs union, rejoining the 
single market, or even a second referendum on membership. Any reset would require 
negotiation, political trade-offs, and time before benefits materialise. But it could bring 
significant economic upside, so signals towards improved trading links could start to lift 
optimism on the UK’s growth prospects.

Exhibit 14: Well-Placed to Benefit from AI Adoption

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, IMF, Bank of England

Exhibit 15: Signals Towards Closer EU Ties Could Boost Sentiment on Long-Run Growth

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Various Academic Studies, Dhingra and Sampson (2019)

Document 344: GLOBAL MARKET CALL_ Stocks Are Cheaper Everywhere

Source PDF: all report pdf/GLOBAL MARKET CALL_ Stocks Are Cheaper Everywhere.pdf

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: GLOBAL MARKET CALL: Stocks Are Cheaper Everywhere
Date:    8 hours ago
GLOBAL MARKET CALL: Stocks Are Cheaper Everywhere

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
GLOBAL MARKET CALL:
Stocks Are Cheaper Everywhere
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 22 Nov 2026
View in browser
Photo by Gaël Gaborel - OrbisTerrae / Unsplash

### Page 2

The AI selloff went global last week. Investors fled to government bonds,
sending yields lower almost everywhere. Global profits are still booming,
yet valuations fell further. We see the selloff as a buying opportunity.
South Korean stocks are pricing in an earnings bust that analysts don't
see coming. In Japan, hotter inflation is raising the odds of a BOJ rate
hike. Here's more:
I. Stock Market Performance
Nearly every country ETF fell last week (chart). Taiwan was hit hardest,

down 23.2% in dollars, followed by South Korea down 13.7%. India was
the only gainer, up 2.8%. The US fell 8.6%, slightly less than the All
Country World ex-US ETF's 9.0% decline.
Stay Home and Go Global are now running neck and neck this year. The
ratios of the US MSCI to the All Country World ex-US MSCI, in dollars and
local currency, have been basically flat since the start of this year (chart).
On October 18, we decided to lower Go Global and raise Stay Home to

market weights.

### Page 3

The US MSCI's share of the All Country World MSCI's market cap edged
down to 65.1% from 65.3% last week (chart). Europe's share edged up to
13.7%, and the EM MSCI edged down to 11.1%. Japan's is just 4.9%.
II. Stock Market Earnings & Valuation
US and overseas forward earnings both remain at record highs (chart).
The global stock market selloff hasn't dented analysts' optimistic earnings
estimates so far.

### Page 4

Global profits are still booming. Forward profit margins are at or near
record highs almost everywhere (chart). Canada's is 17.5%, and the US's
is 16.9%. Japan's has climbed to 10.5%, rapidly approaching the EMU's
10.6%. Might profit margins be reflecting greater AI-driven productivity?
Maybe.
Valuations fell sharply again last week. The US MSCI's forward P/E is
down to 17.7 (chart). The EM MSCI trades at just 7.9, its lowest since the
Great Financial Crisis. We said valuations were at or near a bottom. They

went lower. We still think the panic selling will abate soon.

### Page 5

The US valuation premium is narrowing. The All Country World ex-US
MSCI's forward P/E is 11.2 (chart). The gap with the US has narrowed to
6.5 points from a peak of 9.5 in December 2024.
III. Bonds
Bonds rallied worldwide as investors fled stocks (chart). The US 10-year
Treasury yield fell 24bps to 4.92% from 5.16% a week earlier. The UK's
5.25% is still the highest in the group, followed by Australia's 4.96%.
Japan's yield eased to 2.78%.

### Page 6

Eurozone government bond yield spreads over German bunds are
widening again. The spread between French and German 10-year yields
rose to 157.4bps from 154.2bps (chart). Italy's spread is 110.3bps, and
Greece's is 95.9bps. France remains the weak link.
IV. Dollar
The dollar has been a safe haven during the AI selloff. The DXY rose to
103.8, up from an intraday low of 101.1 on November 4 (chart). We've
been expecting the dollar to remain above the lower end of its upward-

sloping channel. So far, so good.

### Page 7

V. Gold & Commodities
Gold's selling panic continues. The price fell to $3,555 per ounce on
Friday, its lowest level since September 2025 (chart). We still expect
support around $3,400. We pushed our $5,000 year-end 2026 target to
the end of 2027. This is not one of our high-conviction forecasts right now.
Commodity prices fell broadly last week (chart). Copper dropped 9.9% to
$5.48 a pound, as it continues to track the AI trade. Brent crude oil eased

to $92.57 a barrel.

### Page 8

VI. Focus On South Korea & Japan
South Korea's stock market has crashed even as its earnings continue to
soar. The South Korea MSCI's forward earnings is up 325.1% ytd, while
its stock price is up just 26.8% (chart). The price was up about 150% in
June. That has cut the forward P/E by 70.2% this year to a mere 3.2, the
lowest on record. Investors are betting analysts' estimates are too high
based on the reasonable concern that a slowdown in the AI buildout will

depress the memory chip prices of Samsung and SK Hynix.
South Korean exports tell a different story. They soared 93.9% y/y in
October to a record, led by shipments to China and the US (chart). We
see the US backlash against data centers as a speed bump, not a
roadblock, for Asia's chipmakers too.

### Page 9

Japan's inflation is running hot again. Headline CPI rose to 2.4% y/y in
October, above the Bank of Japan's 2.0% target (chart). The jump came
largely from the removal of gasoline subsidies.
The BOJ's policy rate is just 1.25% (chart). The odds of a December hike
are around 65%.

### Page 10

Japan's stock market has moved the same way as Korea's, just less
dramatically. Japan's MSCI forward earnings is up 31.3% ytd, but its stock
price is up just 6.6% (chart).

Document 392: Spain’s election promises collide with a shrinking fiscal margin

Source PDF: all report pdf/Spain’s election promises collide with a shrinking fiscal margin.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-23
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

Spain's main political parties, PSOE, PP and Vox, propose
diﬀerent mixes of spending, tax cuts and reform, but the
ﬁnancing behind their pledges remains unclear. Spain has
limited scope to ﬁnd savings from already low public spending,
while its tax burden constrains broad-based cuts. Rising ageing
costs will add further pressure over time
Pablo Muylle
Spanish voters face a snap election on 29 November
Ahead of Spain’s general election on 29 November, the country’s

public ﬁnances are in much better shape than during the
pandemic. The budget deﬁcit has fallen from 9.9% of GDP in 2020
to 2.4% in 2025, while public debt has declined from 119% to
around 100% of GDP. The deﬁcit is now below the euro area
average, although Spain’s debt ratio remains about 15 percentage
points higher.
Spain’s debt ratio remains high, and further reductions would create
more room to respond to future shocks. But debt alone does not

make public ﬁnances unsustainable: ﬁnancing costs, economic

### Page 2

growth, and the primary balance also matter. Under current
conditions, these factors do not suggest that Spain is on a path
towards a ﬁscal crisis. The next government will therefore inherit a
better starting point, but with limited room for permanent
spending increases or tax cuts without corresponding ﬁnancing.
Population ageing will put more pressure on pensions, healthcare
and long-term care. European recovery funding is also nearing its
end, raising questions about how public investment will be

maintained. Defence, infrastructure and public services will
compete for the same resources.
The election programmes add further demands. PSOE’s centre-left
approach combines expanded social support and public
investment with targeted tax increases. PP’s centre-right
programme emphasises tax relief and administrative eﬃciency. Vox
proposes broader tax cuts and more sweeping cuts to public bodies
and subsidies it considers unnecessary.
Each party identiﬁes potential sources of funding, but none

demonstrates that they would be suﬃcient or sustainable enough
to cover all permanent commitments.

remains above the euro area average

### Page 3

Spain is not a big spender by European
standards
In 2024, Spanish government expenditure amounted to 45.4% of
GDP, compared with 49.3% in the euro area. Spain spent 18.6% of
GDP on social protection versus 20.0% in the euro area; 6.4% on
healthcare versus 7.4%; and 4.1% on education versus 4.6%.
Spending was also lower on defence, housing and economic
aﬀairs.
This matters for the election debate because the three main parties
promise some combination of improved services, more investment

and lower taxes. PP and Vox place particular weight on eﬃciency
reforms, administrative savings and cuts to expenditure they
consider ineﬀective. These measures could release resources, but
Spain’s relatively low level of public spending means there may be
less scope for substantial savings.
Lower expenditure does not necessarily mean greater eﬃciency. It
can reﬂect lower wages, shortages, lower beneﬁt coverage or past
underinvestment. Demographics and the division between public

and private provision also matter. Yet lower spending does not
always coincide with weaker outcomes. Spain spends less on
healthcare per person than the euro area average but records fewer
treatable deaths, while pupils achieve similar maths results on
lower spending per pupil (it’s worth noting that outcomes also
reﬂect demographics, private provision and wider socioeconomic
factors). This weakens the assumption that large savings can
readily be extracted from public services that are both unusually

costly and underperforming.
Better procurement, digitalisation, fewer overlapping procedures
and stricter evaluation of subsidies could still reduce costs while
improving services. Spending reviews could also redirect resources
towards more eﬀective programmes. The programmes do not
estimate how much these measures would save, how quickly the
savings would appear or whether they would be suﬃcient to
ﬁnance permanent tax cuts alongside better services.

Spain spends 10.3% of GDP on old-age beneﬁts, compared with
10.9% in the euro area. This is approximately 10 times the amount
spent on family and children beneﬁts: around 1.0% of GDP in Spain,
compared with 1.9% in the euro area.
Spain spends less on social protection,
healthcare and education
Source: Eurostat COFOG, ING Economic Research
Three ﬁscal strategies, none fully quantiﬁed
PSOE combines additional spending with targeted revenue
increases. It proposes raising the top tax rate on capital income to

35% above €300,000, doubling the ﬁnancial-transactions tax to
0.4%, and increasing taxation of major technology companies and
selected high-income or luxury activities.
These revenues would partly ﬁnance expanded family support,
including a refundable child-related ﬁscal instrument, universal
education and care for children aged zero to three, and a
progressive child-rearing beneﬁt.
PSOE also commits to reducing deﬁcit and debt ratios and
protecting investment as recovery funding ends. Its programme

therefore recognises the ﬁscal constraint, but does not provide a
consolidated calculation showing how its revenues would cover its
broader spending and tax commitments.

### Page 5

PP places greater emphasis on tax relief. Its programme includes
higher personal and family allowances, lower taxation for low- and
middle-income households, and an income-tax exemption for
minimum-wage earners. It also proposes adjusting key income-tax
parameters for inﬂation, temporary zero VAT on basic foods and
substantial relief during the ﬁrst four qualifying tax years for eligible
workers and self-employed people aged 18-35.
PP has set out how it plans to ﬁnd the necessary funding, including

an initial audit of expenditure, subsidies, procurement and public
bodies; fewer ministries and senior oﬃcials; annual spending
reviews; digitalisation; stronger action against fraud; and
withdrawal of funding from ineﬀective programmes.
It also prioritises essential services and maintenance and makes
new expenditure conditional on ﬁnancing. Its commitments,
however, include improvements in childcare, primary healthcare
and dependency care as well as tax cuts. The programme does not

quantify the net savings its reforms would deliver or show how far
they would cover these competing objectives.
Vox proposes tax reductions across a broad range of categories,
including personal income tax, VAT and corporate taxation,
alongside lower contributions for the self-employed and child-
related relief. It also proposes selected revenue increases, including
a remittance levy and taxes on certain housing transactions.
The programme expects stronger growth and cuts to administrative

expenditure, subsidies and public organisations it considers
unnecessary to help ﬁnance its plans. It does not quantify the
revenue cost of its tax cuts, achievable expenditure savings, the
timing of adjustment or the overall ﬁscal impact.
All three parties acknowledge the ﬁnancing constraint, but none
provides a complete numerical reconciliation between its
permanent commitments and the proposed revenue or savings.
The case for broad tax cuts is less clear

than it appears

### Page 6

Spain’s taxes and social contributions raised approximately 38.1%
of GDP in 2025, compared with 41.3% in the euro area. On this
aggregate measure, Spain is not a high-tax country.
But the composition matters. Most of the diﬀerence between Spain
and the euro area comes from taxes on consumption and
production and from social contributions. Personal and corporate
income-tax receipts are broadly in line with the euro area average,
at around 9.6% and 3.2% of GDP respectively.

These ﬁgures provide little evidence that Spain faces a general
problem of excessive taxation, although they do not rule out
targeted reductions where rates are distortionary or particular
groups face an unusually heavy burden. Aggregate receipts say
little about statutory or eﬀective rates or how the burden is
distributed. Broad permanent cuts would still require durable
expenditure savings, oﬀsetting revenue, a broader tax base or
acceptance of a weaker ﬁscal balance.

This is particularly relevant for PP and Vox, whose programmes
combine lower taxation with improvements in services, support for
families and investment. Stronger growth could recover part of the
lost revenue, but the programmes do not provide enough
information to judge whether these eﬀects would be suﬃciently
large. Financing permanent tax cuts through expected growth shifts
the risk to the deﬁcit if the growth or revenue gains fall short.
Spain’s lower tax take mainly reﬂects

consumption taxes and social contributions

Source: Eurostat, ING Economic Research
Ageing will narrow the available ﬁscal room
Spain currently has around 31 people aged 65 or over for every 100
people aged 15 to 64, slightly below the EU ratio of 34. By 2050,
Spain’s old-age dependency ratio is projected to reach 59,
compared with 50 in the EU.
The eventual cost will depend on employment, migration,
productivity, wages, retirement behaviour and future pension
reforms. Even so, maintaining pension adequacy will become more

demanding just as parties seek to improve services, support
families, increase investment and, in some cases, lower taxes.
Both PSOE and PP promise to maintain the purchasing power of
pensions through inﬂation indexation. Vox says it wants to ensure
the pension system’s long-term sustainability but does not explain
what this would entail. None of the parties therefore present
pension cuts as a source of ﬁscal savings, even as ageing increases
pressure on expenditure.

This reduces the scope for ﬁnancing other promises through
general spending restraint. If pension expenditure rises while the
government also protects public services and investment, more of
the adjustment will have to come from tax revenue, cuts elsewhere
or slower debt reduction.
Spain faces a sharp increase in old-age
dependency

Source: Eurostat, ING Economic Research
Investment creates another trade-off
Government ﬁxed investment has risen from around 2% of GDP in
2016-17 to approximately 3% in 2025, supported by European
recovery funding. It remains below the euro area average of around
3.7%. Maintaining investment as recovery funding winds down will
require domestic resources, further European support or more
private participation.
All three parties want to improve infrastructure and productive

capacity. PSOE’s Fondo España Crece aims to mobilise €120bn
through public and public-private instruments, while PP proposes
mobilising more than €300bn alongside private capital over 15
years. Vox lists substantial infrastructure ambitions without an
aggregate amount.
Investment is often relatively easy to postpone during ﬁscal
consolidation. Using it as a balancing item, however, would conﬂict
with ambitions to improve infrastructure, productivity and industrial

capacity.
Spain’s public investment has recovered but
remains below the euro area average

The trade-offs remain
With PP currently ahead of PSOE in the polls, a PP-led government
supported by Vox is a scenario worth considering. Such a
government would prioritise tax relief and expenditure restraint,
although limited ﬁscal room would constrain implementation. The
election is therefore unlikely to change Spain’s near-term economic
outlook substantially. Durable ﬁscal stability will depend more on
pension, labour-market and productivity-enhancing reforms, which

receive limited attention in the programmes.
The next government has some room to change priorities, improve
eﬃciency and reform taxation. But Spain’s relatively low
expenditure suggests that the scope for large savings may be
limited, while permanent tax cuts require equally permanent
ﬁnancing.
Savings can support new commitments only if parties identify the
programmes concerned and quantify achievable amounts. If
revenue, growth or eﬃciency gains fall short, the choices are higher

taxes, more limited commitments, lower investment or slower debt
reduction.

Document 428: WEEKLY WEBCAST_ Slicing & Dicing The Fed’s Inflation Problem

Source PDF: all report pdf/WEEKLY WEBCAST_ Slicing & Dicing The Fed’s Inflation Problem.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-18
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: WEEKLY WEBCAST: Slicing & Dicing The Fed’s Inflation Problem
Date:    3 hours ago
WEEKLY WEBCAST: Slicing & Dicing The Fed’s Inflation Problem

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
WEEKLY WEBCAST: Slicing &
Dicing The Fed’s Inflation
Problem
By Ed Yardeni • 18 Nov 2026
View in browser

When inflation is driven by supply shocks, there’s less the Fed can do
about it. But today’s inflation is not caused totally by supply shocks, won’t
simply snap back to the Fed’s target rate when the shocks dispel, and can
be influenced by monetary policy. Today, Elias and Ed share their belief
that the inflationary environment changed structurally in the years after the
pandemic, in ways that make it more difficult for the Fed to achieve its
inflation target. Without being able to influence structurally higher goods

### Page 2

inflation as much, the Fed will have to lower services inflation by raising
the federal funds rate. We think 50 more basis points will do it. Lower oil
prices should help to moderate inflation in the near term; productivity
should do so in the long run.

components are turning down quite

• Fuel price swings continue to have a
significantly influence. Excluding fuel,
quarterly growth has seen a more
material slowing from 1.9%qtr in
September to 1%qtr in October and early

November. A lift in price inflation more
generally conceals a likely much softer
picture in real terms.
• The rise in fuel spend is clearly displacing
spend on other categories. Growth
in discretionary activity in particular
has slowed to a crawl, tracking at just
0.4%qtr in the latest week with the last
six weeks showing a particularly abrupt
pull-back in discretionary services.
The more granular detail shows small
outright declines for accommodation &

gambling, personal & other services, and
transport services.
• The October CPI update on November
25 will give a better indication of the
price-volume mix of growth in the
latest month. The next round of official
spending measures – the September
quarter national accounts on December
2 and the October ABS household
spending indicator on December 3 – are
both likely to show reasonable gains
although we suspect the December
quarter will reveal a more pronounced

slowing in real spending once they are
released.
“Quarterly growth remains
relatively firm ... [but] the
detail is a lot less convincing.”

4
• Large fuel price swings continue to
complicate the spending picture. Chart
4 shows card activity split into fuel,
electricity and other segments combined,
Chart 5 splitting the latter out into
essentials, discretionary goods and
discretionary services respectively.

• While energy only makes up around 7%
of total card activity, the swings in this
component have been large this year. In
growth terms, the rollercoaster saw an
initial peak around 15%qtr in May, then a
big dipper that bottomed out at –6.6%qtr
in August and then a second peak around
16%qtr in November that again looks to
be turning lower (albeit at a much slower
pace).
• Energy accounts for 0.5ppt of the current
1.7%qtr growth in the headline index. The

1.2%qtr momentum ex energy is even
slower since the start of Oct, tracking a
quarterly pace of just 0.2%qtr.

spend on other categories. Growth in
discretionary activity in particular has
slowed to a crawl, tracking at just 0.4%qtr
in the latest week with the last six weeks
showing a particularly abrupt pull-back in
discretionary services. The more granular
detail shows small outright declines for
accommodation & gambling, personal &

other services, and transport services.  In
contrast, non-energy essentials spend has
been much steadier.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS 9
About the Westpac card data indicators
The indicators presented in this report are based on the millions of credit and debit card
transactions processed every day. Transactions covering over ten million merchants are
classified into over 700 categories. These are in turn grouped into higher level aggregates

that provide a timely guide to wider economic trends.
The main focus of these indicators is consumer spending. Where possible, we have sought
to exclude ‘non spending’ transactions such as: money transfers; tax payments; loan
repayments; charitable donations; and superannuation contributions.
It should also be noted that these indicators will also be affected by shifts between card and
non-card transactions. This was a significant factor during the COVID–19 pandemic – health

concerns about the use of physical cash leading to significantly higher use of cards vs cash,
particularly where contact–less transactions are available. Transaction flows also include
reversals/refunds which were also a significant phenomenon during the onset of COVID,
especially in areas such as travel.
All transaction data is compiled at a highly aggregated level so that individual customer or
merchant data is never revealed.
Index construction

The key metrics used in this report are indexes of spending–related card activity where the
base of 100 is average activity in 2019. As an example, if transaction flows are 5% above their
average level in 2019, the index read for the period is 105. If flows in a subsequent period are
8% above the average level in 2019, the index read for this period is 108. Growth between the
two periods can be calculated simply as the change between the two index reads, i.e. 2.9%.

All measures are adjusted for regular seasonality. Weekly estimates are generated using
the US Bureau of Labor’s MoveReg weekly seasonal adjustment program. Note that in some
cases, high levels of volatility during the COVID period mean it is not possible to produce
seasonally adjusted estimates for some historical periods.
Also, note that previous versions of this report used different approaches to seasonal
adjustment and measurement more generally. This means Index reads are sometimes not

directly comparable. See the ‘About the Westpac card data indicators’ sections from earlier
reports to more detail.
Classifications
Note that the measures used for card data and in this report do not align completely with
the those used in official ABS statistics, including the ABS household spending indicator,
ABS retail trade survey and ABS estimate of consumer spending published in the national
accounts. There are a range of differences including around both coverage and classification.

As such, the card data should be treated as broadly indicative.
The transaction data is grouped into 26 categories that are then combined into four main as
follows:
Discretionary goods: alcoholic beverages, tobacco, clothing & footwear, furnishings &
household equipment, household appliances, vehicle–related, recreational & cultural goods,
newspaper, books & stationery, and other personal effects.
Discretionary services: transport services (part), recreational & cultural services, gambling,

catering services, accommodation services, other personal care, insurance & financial
services, other services.
Essential goods: food & non alcoholic beverages, medical products, appliances & equipment,
and operation of personal transport equipment.
Essential services: electricity, gas & other fuels, health services, transport services (part),
communications and education.
The report also uses two additional classifications:
MHSI/Retail: based on the extent to which categories cover activity that is in scope for the

ABS monthly household spending indicator and ABS retail trade survey.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
It has been an action-packed few weeks for markets. The US
2026 mid-term elections saw a repudiation of President Trump
with the Democrats now set to take control over both houses
of Congress. The outcome and easing Middle East tensions

saw Brent crude oil fall from just under US$120/bbl to roughly
US$95/bbl, back around the post-Iran-conflict average. The
associated easing in inflation fears has supported a continued
descent in bond yields and rally in risk sentiment – albeit with
significant ‘wrinkles’ around a high US inflation read and an
AI-led pull-back in equity markets that has been compounded
by the sudden resignation of NVIDIA’s CEO.
Many central banks have already begun re-tightening policy

– with the Bank of Canada and Bank of England the latest
additions to the pack – but what comes next will differ widely
across jurisdictions. In the US, the combination of strong
activity and sticky inflation have led FOMC members to more
strongly advocate for further tightening. We have added a
January hike to the December move already in our forecasts,
before a long pause and eventual cuts in 2028.
Europe shares has similar inflation concerns but not so much

strength on the growth front. The ECB sees activity near
trend and is uncomfortable with inflation at 3.8%yr, so we
expect a December hike as insurance. Unlike market pricing
though, we think this will be the last, with energy and
investment-related price pressures easing through 2027 and
scope to ease back towards neutral in 2028. China faces a
very different challenge – not too much demand but too little.
Industrial strength and technology investment are providing

support but we expect policy direction and stimulus will be
needed to turn household demand, with this expected to
come in 2027 and property the most likely catalyst.
The RBA meanwhile followed its September hike with another
in November, but the Governor’s call to ‘take stock’ signals a
December pause and we think the hiking phase is now more
clearly over. We continue to pencil in a move to cuts in the
second half of 2027. While a February hike is not completely

out of the question, policy is clearly restrictive and downside
risks are crystallising. Reflecting this, we have downgraded
our GDP, labour market and housing market forecasts for 2027.
Australia: The housing downturn has been sharper than
expected and is set to spill over more materially to the broader
economy. The scale of these spillovers should become clearer
over coming months, keeping the RBA on hold as downside
risks to growth build. Business investment remains a source

of resilience, led by data centres and renewables, but rising
costs and growing community opposition have seen some
projects shelved. Still, the supply-side picture is constructive,
with strong investment in the market sector driving capital
deepening and productivity gains, albeit uneven ones.
Commodities: The commodities complex was a story of two
halves in October, with sharp gains across energy offsetting
weakness elsewhere and lifting our broadest commodities

index 0.6%mth. Reflecting continued energy-market tightness,
we expect Brent to average US$101/bbl in Q4 and have raised
our Q4 Japanese LNG forecast to US$27.
Global FX Markets: Of late, risks have favoured the US
dollar and FOMC member concerns have been decidedly one-
sided towards higher inflation. The DXY has consequently
continued to test the upper limit of its 18-month range, but
remains inside it.
New Zealand: A busy month has included a tight – and

currently inconclusive – general election, a surprise hold by the
RBNZ in October and a somewhat disappointing Q3 labour
market report. However, the medium-term outlook remains little
changed, with a recovering economy and above-target inflation
likely to lead to further monetary tightening in coming months.
Pacific: Distance from major markets, small domestic
economies and a heavy reliance on imported fuel and food
mean global disruptions tend to reach the Pacific at a higher

cost. However, most Pacific countries face the latest shock
with somewhat better buffers.
United States: Data suggests the US carried strong
momentum in to H2 2026. But questions are being asked over
the profitability of AI deployment, household real incomes are
trending flat to down, and term interest rates continue to weigh.
China: China GDP surprised to the upside in Q3, keeping
authorities on track for 2026, but more importantly offering a
little more time to accelerate domestic demand in to 2027.

Asia: We expect the BoJ to raise its policy rate by 25bps to
1.50% in December, before gradually taking it to 2.0% through
2027. The case for further tightening rests on the evidence that
firms are more willing to raise prices, labour scarcity supports
persistent wage growth, which should in turn support spending.
Europe & UK: The ECB left its key policy rates unchanged in
October. Another hike is forecast in December. Against market
pricing, we expect this will prove the last for the current cycle.

Taking stock
